Will knows that Hannibal helped Abigail to dispose of the body but the reason he doesn’t go to Jack is because he hopes to be wrong about it. Will tells Hannibal that Abigail killed Nicholas and Hannibal admits that he is aware of that. Hannibal is now exposed. So he needs to make a judgment call. He then decides to try and explain the situation to Will, anticipating that he would be able to persuade him to keep the secret. Hannibal explains that everything he said to the FBI about that night was true except for one thing. Nicholas attacked them, but Abigail killed him to protect herself. Hannibal knew what was going to happen to her if Jack ever found out about it (Jack was already suspecting Abigail to be connected to her father’s crimes, so this would only make it worse for her). That along with the fact that people and the families of the victims would burn her for her father’s crimes. Hannibal tries  to appeal to Will's fatherhood instincts (that Will has created for Abigail because of his personality disorder), saying that Abigail is their responsibility now and they should protect her at all costs. Hannibal also tries to use Will's empathy against him by connecting Abigail’s situation with Will’s experience of killing Hobbs and Hannibal's experiences of killing Tobias. At the end of this conversation Will describes keeping the secret for Abigail’s sake but also for Hannibal’s. With this revelation to Will, Hannibal was not only able to further manipulate Will but he also made him a part of his crime, forcing him to lie to Jack and everyone else and reinforce his relationship to him (by cutting him off from everyone else). By doing this Hannibal had also created a stronger connection with Will.
(Logical + Emotional Manipulation, Information Control, Decision Making, Anticipation, Persuasion, EU)

-After that, Hannibal organizes a dinner with Will and Freddy about Abigail’s story to the public. Abigail is able to feel that something has changed with Will. So after the dinner, when Abigail and Hannibal are alone, she asks Hannibal if Will knows about Nicholas. Hannibal replies by saying that he knows but the secret is safe. He is able to anticipate that Will will not only keep the secret, but he will also lie to Jack and everyone because he knows that the most important thing in Will’s life is Abigail. Hannibal also knows Abigail's dark secret, a secret that she won’t even admit to herself and that she will do anything to keep it inside. Hannibal knows that Abigail was helping her father to catch the girls he was killing and he even makes Abigail admit it to him on the spot. After that Hannibal comforts her, further manipulating her by giving her the things she lacks most in life. Which is family.
(STP, Anticipation, Emotional Manipulation)

 Episode Summary - Will starts to experience loss of time for the first time when he visits murder scenes. At this episode Hannibal is trying to create the idea in Will’s mind that: 1) all of his symptoms are related to the high pressure and the overwhelming use of his personality disorder, which Jack abuses 2) Will’s memory loss is caused by his overwhelming emotional state when he’s at a crime scene (connecting his condition with the view of the scene) 3) his condition is not neurological but psychological and that Will is just trying to avoid it by searching for solutions other than psychotherapy 4) If Will continues to operate that way he might turn himself into a killer (the people Will is literally hunting for). Hannibal is also the one that is creating Will’s memory loses in the first place (S2 spoilers). After manipulating Will and understanding his feelings about Abigail, Hannibal also decides to share crucial information with him about Abigail, knowing that Will would be forced to keep it in order to save her life and by extension, forcing Will to come closer to Hannibal by cutting him off from the rest of his colleagues. That way Hannibal could continue to manipulate Will in the future.
(Strategy, Anticipation, Psychological/Emotional
Manipulation, Persuasion)

S1 Ep 10

-The episode starts with the murder of a girl in a house.

-Then we go to another one of Hannibal’s sections with Will in Hannibal’s office. Will express to Hannibal his feelings about his worsening condition, saying that he’s close to the breaking point. He then goes on to remind Hannibal that Abigail killed some and that they lie for her, implying that he’s not past the fact of Abigail’s actions. Hannibal points out that Will is grieving, but not for the life he himself has taken in the past, but the life that was taken from him because of his condition. Will feels like he's not in control of his life anymore and that’s why he expresses those emotions in the actions of Abigail. Hannibal states that if Abigail is capable of leaving behind the madness and the murder and starting over then so is Will (giving him hope to continue the therapy). Knowing that Will wouldn’t be satisfied with this, Hannibal asks him about his symptoms, mainly if he had experienced any further loss of time or hallucinations, to which Will answered yes. Then Hannibal decides to ask Will to draw a clock as an exercise to better understand the reality around him. Will draws the clock and for him is a normal clock, but in reality the clock is completely out of place (showing that special neglect has appeared as another of Will’s symptoms). But even after Hannibal sees the clock that Will drew and how desmorft it is, he’s not faced at all, instead he acts like everything is although and lets Will believe that he has indeed drawn a normal clock (concealing Will’s symptom from Will himself). By doing that Hannibal achieves 2 things: 1) he continues to persuade Will that his symptoms are not neurological but psychological by concealing the symptoms that imply a neurological disorder. And 2) he gives Will fake scenes of control and misdirects his mind about the situation and what’s causing it. The reason Hannibal wants Will to draw this clock is not only to give Will a fake scene of control as his symptoms are getting worse but also to use it against Will in the future, if anyone suspects that Will’s condition might be neurological.
(Strategy, Manipulation: Gaslighting, EM, Composure, Deception: Misdirection and Acting Skills, Anticipation, Psychological Manipulation)

-Afterwards, we can see Will returning from fishing. He goes inside his house and into the kitchen, putting the fishes he coats on top of the kitchen table. After opening one up with a knife he sees an unrealistic amount of blood flowing through the fish (Will has another hallucination episode). Will blinks and suddenly he finds himself in the crime scene of the girl that was murdered at the beginning of the episode. Will finds himself on top of the victim as she is slowly dying. Will doesn’t know where he is or how he got there. He has bloody hands and holds a knife. Will jumps terrified (believing that he had just killed a person), leaving the body on the floor and opening the door when he sees Jack and the FBI team outside the room , waiting for Will to finish his usual analysis of the crime scene. Will is shocked, and so is the rest of the team.

-Will immediately goes to wash his hands from the blood. He then goes outside the house with Jack and he asks Will about what happened there.
Jack states that he has never seen Will that way before, he had seen Will apset before but not scared. Will tries to act normal and explain himself to Jack, saying that he just got lost in the reconstruction of the scene. Jack reminds Will that he had never interfered with the crime scene that way before. Will explains that he felt like he killed her and that’s what got him disturbed in there. Jack is officially worried about Will’s condition, believing that he might have pushed Will too far. Will notes that even so, Jack has no one else that can do this job better than him unbroken.

-After the crime scene has been properly analyzed by Will, he returns to Hannibal’s office to tell him about the incident. Will says that he has no prior memories about the crime scene. The only thing that he can remember is himself cutting off the victim’s face and killing her, even though that would be impossible because the victim was already dead, but it all felt so real for Will. Hannibal states that Will must learn to differentiate reality from his delusions. But Will knows himself. He knows that he’s not that kind of crazy and that something is wrong with him (he suspects something neurological), despite Hannibal’s beast efforts to misdirect him. So Hannibal decides to take Will for a brain scan, he even offers him a neurologist he knows. But he tells Will that if it turns out that his condition isn’t neurological, Will would have to accept that it’s probably a mental illness.
(Decision Making, Improvisation, Persuasion)

-So Hannibal takes Will to the neurologist. The Doctor knows Hannibal personally and even describes him as one of the sanest men he knows (Hannibal’s person suit). He knew Hannibal from since he was also in the medical field and operating as a surgeon. But Hannibal says that he was always more interested in how the mind works instead of how the brain works. We learn that Will’s headaches started 3 months ago, then Will decided to return to the field (creating the idea that Will symptoms are indeed related with his work and his disorder). But Will isn’t aware at what point the hellucinations started, he just realized that he might not be dreaming. So Will is admitted for a brain scan.
(Knowledge, Deception: Charisma + Acting Skills)

-While Will is in a room, doing his brain scan, Hannibal and the Doctor are in another room observing his results. Hannibal admits to his friend that he suspects the condition to be encephalitis. When his friend asks Hannibal why he thinks that Hannibal answers that he smelled it. Not only that but Hannibal’s sense of smell is so strong that he could even describe how an autoimmune disease smells with extreme detail.
His friend says “you have gone from smelling nurses perfumes to diagnosing autoimmune diseases?” meaning that Hannibal doesn’t display his full abilities when he’s in public or in his social life (because even his friend, who knew Hannibal from medical school wasn’t aware that Hannibal had that sense of smell). Hannibal then proceeds showing the Doctor Will’s clock drawing and his special neglect while also describing Will’s other symptoms like the hellucinations and the timeline that they occurred. The reason Hannibal is trying to persuade the Doctor that Will has encephalitis is to offer him a chance to study a person with that condition from up close. You see Hannibal knows that this Doctor would do anything for his field including crossing some lines. So he uses his charisma to try and persuade him to fake the results of Will’s tests so that both him and Hannibal could further “study” the symptoms of this condition. “It’s very rare to be able to study the psychological effects on this type of condition in a person’s mind”. “A Doctor has to wave the ultimate benefit of scientific study, even to this day we know so little about the brain”.
After the results are confirmed, we see that Will indeed has encephalitis and that his symptoms are only going to get worse, which Hannibal knows. But Hannibal doesn’t care, for him Will’s condition only makes him a lab rat for Hannibal to study. So after the test is done the Doctor tells Will that they didn’t find anything wrong with him neurological, which only leaves the possibility of a psychological problem. Thus, Will is faced with his biggest fear, which is the fact that he’s slowly losing his mind to the crime scenes and the criminals that he analyzes.
(Fabrication, Charisma, Persuasion, Acting Skills, Strategy, Information Control, Anticipation, Perception)

-At the same night, Jack and Hannibal are talking about Will in Hannibal’s house. Hannibal states the fact that Jack knew from the moment he set foot in Will’s classroom that he might put Will in a potentially destructive environment. But Jack says that Will was the only person who could catch the killer who had killed 8 young girls. Hannibal goes on to say that after they didn’t find any neurological problems with him, Will was very upset, because he wanted an answer that wasn’t mental illness. Jack asks Hannibal if he thinks that Will is mentally ill and Hannibal goes on to explain to Jack how Will's personal disorder works from a psychological perspective. He explains that the problem Will has is too many mirror neurons in his mind, our brains are full of them when we were children, they’re supposed to help us socialize and then they melt away as we get older. But in Will’s case they didn’t, which makes Will knowing who he is a challenge. Hannibal goes on to say that when Will is visiting a crime scene, his mind does not only reflect the mind of the killer, but he also absorbs it. Essentially Hannibal is trying to create a picture of Will’s condition in which Will is extremely susceptible to being influenced by any kind of crime scene that Jack is pushing him into.
(Abstraction, Verbal Deception, Manipulation)

-With Will now believing that his condition is psychological or mental illness he revisits the crime scene in hopes of beating his condition or better understanding it. He goes inside the house, in the room where the girl had died. After seeing the blood on the floor Will repeats the words that Hannibal had advised him to say when he is at a vulnerable position (the time , place and his name). After that Will is able to observe a strange girl under the bed. Without knowing if this is another hallucination or not Will tries to get a better look at that girl under the bed, but as he tries to look down the mysterious girl runs away while hitting Will in the process. Will holds her hand but the skin just slides off. Immediately afterwards Will finds himself in the middle of the forest outside of the victims house. He looks at his clock and time has passed. Will lost time again after visiting the crime scene. This almost confirms that his condition is indeed related with the crimes and the killers.
(Orchestrated by Hannibal/Strategy S2)

-Immediately afterwards, Will called Beverly (a member of his FBI team) to search the place because Will wasn’t entirely sure that what he saw was real. Will describes his experience about the girl running away, but before that Will states that when he grabbed her arm an entire layer of dead skin was separated like she was wearing a glove. That’s why she didn’t bleed when the victim scratched her. But Will can’t remember what he did with the layer of skin. But he is able to deduce her condition. Her condition is really serious, she's malnourished, her liver is failing and Will thinks she's mutilated the victim's face because she's seen it as a mask, she can't distinguish faces, which means she may not be aware of it and that would explain why she's come back, to check if she really has. After the analysis, Beverly says to Will that he is the suspect of a lot of speculation in the bureau. Some people say that Jack pushed Will to the edge and now he’s pushing himself over. Which means that people have already started to see the cracks in Will’s personality.

-After that we see Will in Hannibal’s office. Hannibal has asked Will to draw a clock again and Will does. The clock is still out of place but Hannibal acts like it’s normal by not responding to the abnormalities of the clock. Hannibal tries to make Will think that they’ve made progress by saying to him that he didn’t lose his sanity when he saw the killer. But Will states that he still lost time. Hannibal says that he and his friend will continue to search for answers until they find something. In the meantime, Hannibal suspects that the killer might have Cotard's Syndrome, which is a rare delusional disorder in which a person believes that he or she is dead. Cotard’s is also associated with someone’s inability to see others or recognise their faces. Is essentially a misfiring in the areas of the brain which recognise faces. So this syndrome is covering all of the symptoms of the killer and Hannibal believes that the killer has it.
(Psychological and Medical Knowledge, Deduction, Manipulation)

-After a few days, Will returns to the clinic to run some more tests. Will is put on the MRI scan by the Doctor and then let alone. When Will awakes the scan is complete, Will finds himself alone in the room without the Doctor. He gets out of the room but it seems like there is no other person in this hospital, so Will goes to the Doctors office. When he finds his office he can see blood on the doorknob. He opens the door carefully and finds the Doctor dead in his desk, with his face cut off in a similar way with the victim they found in the forest (the girl). The FBI arrives on the scene, Will believes that he might be the one who killed the Doctor but his team can’t find even a single evidence on him. But they found the murder weapon, with the same dead skin tissue as the first crime scene. But the only connection between the first and the second crime scenes is Will. So Will suspects that the killer might have followed him to the hospital and killed the Doctor because she can’t recognise faces. Why would she follow Will? Well Will suspects that she might believe that Will was the only one who could understand her because he reminded her that she was alive (she didn’t think of that because of the Cotard's Syndrome). We later learned that the real killer behind the murder was Hannibal.

-Later that night, at 00:00, Will finds the same person in his house, under his bed. Will explains to her the situation, where she is, what time it is, and that she's alive and well (because she has  Cotard's Syndrome). That way Will is able to calm her down and the FBI takes her to a hospital because of her condition. Because of her cut off skin and her bad immune system there is a serious risk of infection. So she is in a ventilator and treated like a fire victim.

-After the FBI has finally caught their killer, Jack visits Hannibal again to talk about the case. Hannibal is worried about Will. Jack thinks that Hannibal would be more upset about the loss of his friend, but Hannibal says that although he’s grieving for the Doctor, Will is very much alive and in need of their help. Jack wants to interrogate the girl about the case when she wakes up, but Hannibal remarks that “for her sake I hope she doesn’t remember much”.

Episode Summary - Will’s symptoms are getting extremely worse every day, and Will is in need of some answers. Hannibal, knowing that Will wouldn’t be satisfied with his theories, decides to get Will to a friend of his that he’s also a neuroscientist (known that his friend wouldn’t have any problem withholding information from a client for the sake of his research). So Hannibal is able to persuade his friend to keep the results of Will’s tests a secret and to also lie to him about the results (while at the same time Hannibal is concealing some of Will’s symptoms like the spatial neglect from Will himself). All that made Will believe that he was indeed losing his mind to the crime scenes he analyzed. After Will had believed Hannibal’s theory, Hannibal sneaked into the hospital while Will was having an MRI scan and killed his friend in a similar way that the girl had killed the first victim but not quite. The way Hannibal killed his friend was similar but this time he had gone too far with the mutilation (to create the idea that this might be a different killer that is just imitating the original). The reason Hannibal did that is 1) so that no one else would know about Will’s condition other than Hannibal and 2) so that he could use this murder to frame Will in the future. But after killing the Doctor he was interrupted by the girl . So Hannibal just parted her the murder weapon and left without leaving a single piece of evidence. Leaving the FBI to assume that Will might be the killer.
(Strategy, Anticipation, Planning, Misdirection, Fabrication, Tactics, Improvisation, Manipulation)

S1 Ep 11

- Gideon manages to escape and threatens a psychologist to call Freddie Lounds about Gideon's escape. In the call, the psychologist says he wants to work with Freddie Lounds on an article about Gideon, so they have to meet. When Freddie Lounds arrives, we see Gideon and he has killed the psychologist.

- Freddie and Gideon go to the place where Miriam Lass's arm with a telephone had been found, Freddie explains that she wrote an article about the Chesapeake Ripper to provoke him and that he responded to the provocation by putting Miriam's arm with a telephone there.

- Some time after Hannibal kills someone by removing their arm, Will will instantly deduces that his objective is to say that Gideon is at this location, where they're found an arm before. How did he know? When the police found the psychologist's body, Gideon asked for his blood to be delivered to the Red Cross in preparation for his first meeting with Hannibal, but more importantly, when Will touched the psychologist's hand, an article about the death appeared on the office computer, confirming to everyone that he had kidnapped Freddie Lounds. Then Freddie Lounds wrote an article about Gideon as the Chesapeake Ripper to provoke Hannibal into coming to the place where he'd been provoked the first time, but Hannibal didn't show up. But Hannibal immediately saw the trap so he didn’t risk it nor lost his composure but instead he gave the information about Gideon’s location to the FBI by using a body. His motive to send the FBI after Gideon instead of going after him himself was also to further push Will psychologically, Hannibal was aware of Will’s deteriorating mental health and wanted to use this opportunity to put him against Gideon.
(Trap Evasion, Information Gathering, Creative Thinking, Composure, Planning)

- He called the police. When they arrived, Gideon had already left. Gideon gets into a police car and finds Will going more and more crazy. Will starts hallucinating and instead of Gideon Will sees Hobbs (an idea that Hannibal had planned into Will’s mind since the first episode). Because of Will's condition and his psychological state those last months, Will doesn’t trust his senses and perception of reality, so instead of calling the FBI for assistance, Will instead asks Gideon (who he perceives as Hobbs) to drive him to Hannibal house at gunpoint. Once at Hannibal's, he tries to calm him down and explain the situation, except that Will is hallucinating, not seeing Gideon but Garret Jacob Hobbs. Hannibal decides to tell Will that there is no one else in the room except the two of them, and that what he sees isn’t real. Will finds it very difficult to believe, but Hannibal’s permission is too much, Will in his panic really believes that he’s losing his mind and in a state of high stress he has a minor seizure.
(Anticipation, Planning, Manipulation, Persuasion)

- Now with Will in a catatonic state, Hannibal and Gideon are alone, Hannibal will start talking about the fact that Gideon doesn't know he's the Chesapeake Ripper, even though he knows he's not. Because, remember, Chilton mentally manipulated him into believing he was the Chesapeake Ripper.
(Info Control, Strategy)

These events were anticipated by Hannibal; if they weren't, he'd never have mentioned Alana's name, never have left the gun on the table, and his real objective, as we recall, is to incriminate Will, who has just shot someone, so…
Hannibal indirectly manipulated Will while Helping Gideon at the same time

The Framing of Will
A Summary of Hannibal’s Strategy to Frame Will in Season 1:

Hannibal’s strategy to frame Will was the following: It all started in the first episode of the series, when Hannibal met Will for the first time. Hannibal was able to immediately understand Will’s condition and empathize with him. It was at their first meeting that Hannibal decided that Will was the perfect person to use as an escape route if the FBI came too close to him, because of his rare condition (being someone who could literally think like a killer because of his empathy). After their first meeting Hannibal decided to help Will catch Hobbs by creating a crime scene that would make Will indirectly look into the mind of Hobbs. After he helped Will to locate Hobbs he warned Hobbs about the situation because he knew how he would react by perfectly understanding him. He intentionally put Will in a position where he was forced to kill Hobbs in order to save Abigail. Hannibal wanted Will to empathize with Hobbs in a great way for 3 reasons: 1) Hannibal knew how Hobbs would react and he wanted to show to the world that Will was capable of murder 2) He wanted to create a strong connection between Abigail and Will via Hobbs so that he could use her to frame Will in the future. Hannibal wanted to establish that Will was forming the same relationship that Hobbs had with his daughter. A relationship that ended with Hobbs trying to kill Abigail 3) He wanted Will to empathize with Hobbs so much, to make Will feel like he was the one who killed those girls that Hobbs had killed. That way, Hannibal could make Will confess those feelings about the case in one of their sessions and use them as evidence for Will’s framing in the future. This first incident was just the beginning of Hannibal’s strategy to frame Will. By indirectly leading Will into the killer, Hannibal expected that Hobbs would try and kill his family before they captured him, so that Will would be forced to kill Hobbs and show to the world that Will is capable of violence in the first place. In every murder Hannibal committed he was leaving space for the existence of another killer that the original (a copycat) by leaving some subtle differences between the original murder and the one Hannibal was committing. This difference was made to point out to the existence of a single killer that was present in all of those murders (Will Graham) so that even if the FBI is able to connect all of those seemingly unrelated murders, they would find Will and not Hannibal as the killer. Also, from very early on, Hannibal was trying to antagonize Jack to Will in order to make Will distance himself from everyone, allowing Hannibal’s future strategies to be more effective. After the incident with Hobbs, Hannibal also started visiting Will’s house when Will was away and planted physical evide of his crimes in the most hidden places. For Hannibal’s framing of Will to be more successful he needed Abigail. So Hannibal created a strategy to force Abigail into killing a man and then psychologically manipulate her into believing that this was her fault, while also helping her dispose of the body. By doing that, Hannibal forced Abigail to depend on him emotionally, so that he could further use her to isolate Will from the FBI. This happened by manipulating Will and understanding his feelings about Abigail. Hannibal also decided to share crucial information about Abigail’s murder with him, knowing that Will would be forced to keep it in order to save her life and by extension, forcing Will to come closer to Hannibal by cutting him off from the rest of his colleagues. By forcing Will to lie in order to save Abigail’s life he also made Will emotionally dependent on him by putting him in a similar situation. This way Hannibal could continue to manipulate Will in the future. While
Hannibal was trying to isolate Will from the rest of the team, he was also trying to create the image that Will was slowly losing his mind because of his personality disorder (he achieved that by making Will clash with Jack and later by using Will’s condition). Hannibal was able to successfully create the idea in everyone that Will was losing his mind when he was at a crime scene in two ways: 1) As Will’s psychiatrist and an expert in the field, he was able to explain to Jack how Will's ability actually works. He explained that every time Will was at a crime scene he wasn’t only able to reflect the thoughts of the killers but also to absorb them (using Will’s empathy disorder as an argument to make him look more dangerous) and 2) by strategically inducing some of Will’s symptoms like the memory losses by using repeating lights to create seizures, so that when Will was on a crime scene it would look like his theory about Will’s condition was right. The most crucial part of Hannibal’s strategy started after he deduced Will’s Encephalitis. Hannibal had smelled Will’s encephalitis from the very beginning, and was using indirect & direct psychological methods  to worsen it further. To do that, Hannibal was also manipulating Jack by psychologically pushing him as the Chesapeake Ripper (sending him messages of his dead trainee) in order to make him more invested in the case and by doing this, force Jack to use Will more frequently in his cases (worsening Will’s condition). This also created the idea that Jack was the one who pushed Will into losing his mind by forcing him to use his personality disorder in more cases. At times even Hannibal will send Will in dangerous situations (like when he sends Will after Tobias) to expose him to stress and worsen his condition. Hannibal was also concealing Will’s neurological symptoms by hiding his special neglect from him and he was even fabricating the results of his neurological tests to make it look like Will’s condition wasn’t neurological but rather psychological. He did that by persuading the Doctor using his charisma to lie to Will about his condition and then killing the Doctor by making it look like another killer did it (this murder will also be used against Will in the future). As early as episode 4, Hannibal visits Will’s house and scopes out the best available objects in his house to plant the remnants of his victims later on. Will’s worsened mental state entailed spatial negligence, time loss and identity crisis. After he aroused these symptoms, he planned to use them as concrete and plausible evidence for his frame. He did that by 1)Purposefully exposes Will to psychologically stressful situations that naturally worsen his condition, ensuring he becomes more susceptible to manipulation. 2)By acting as Will’s psychiatrist, Hannibal gains the opportunity to steer him away from accurate diagnoses and effective treatments, allowing the symptoms to escalate unchecked, while also remaining in touch with the progress of the FBI’s investigation of his case. 3)Strategically inducing Will’s crises and memory losses by using a form of light therapy in order to gaslight Will and further manipulate him. 4)During their therapy sessions, Hannibal skillfully seeds thoughts and suggestions that contribute to Will's ongoing confusion, thereby exacerbating his condition in a non-medical but deeply impactful way. Hannibal was cultivating him, waiting for Will to talk about how uncertain he was about himself in their session, and how good it felt to kill. Hannibal was precisely waiting for this moment, and vaguely guiding Will into saying these words so that he would be able to record them and use it as evidence for Will’s incrimination later on, to further perfect this frame from every perspective, in order to not be suspected at all. As we know, Will’s time loss was quite significant and repetitive, he was losing a conscious awareness of time, and blacking out at random points, not knowing what nor remembering what he did whilst he was in the blackout state. Will’s memory losses were induced by Hannibal himself to achieve the following goals: 1) To reinforce the idea that Will’s condition was related with the crime scene and his work by controlling the timing of his memory losses. Will would lose his memory only when he was at the present of a crime or when he was trying to empathize with the killer (which Hannibal also used to make Will think that this was all a desperate reaction of his brain to avoid the pressure) making it look like Will was indeed absorbing the killer’s thoughts and feelings. 2) To destroy Will’s alibi at the time of his crisis. When Will lost time he was unable to recall anything, including his actions and whereabouts at the moment of the crisis. This not only made Will scared about his potential actions but also Jack and the FBI. Another way Hannibal was able to frame Will was by concealing his true condition, which was the encephalitis. Hannibal made Will draw a clock to supposedly help him with his psychological trauma, but in reality Hannibal wanted to use this clock to hide Will’s true condition from the rest of the world. Hannibal had already anticipated that Dr. Bloom would test Will for encephalitis, in order to assess his spatial negligence, by making him draw a clock. So, what Hannibal already had done, was sketch a normal clock, and presented it to the FBI as if Will had drawn it himself. He had anticipated that Alana would test Will for encephalitis, and make him sketch a clock, so he took advantage of this forethought, and drew a normal clock in advance, and cloaked it with the lie that Will drew it. These contradicting sketches were to induce and manipulate the law enforcement into thinking another possibility, which was: 1) Will is smart. What if he was faking his assessments, and deluded everyone into thinking he was struck by encephalitis, so that he would later on use it as his alibi for his crimes? This is what Hannibal’s clock brought. Made them consider the possibility, and worsen his confinement condition further. After all these were implemented, it was relatively easier for Hannibal to execute his strategy. He only had to plant physical evidence now. Will’s induced memory losses allow Hannibal to execute the final part of his strategy. Will takes Abigail with him back to the Hobbs’ house with the hope of making her retrieve her previous memories regarding that incident. Will wanted to re-start from the beginning to find new clues about the case, Will was extremely stressed, his mental state was at its worst, triggering a crisis during which Abigail took the opportunity to leave, heading for the Garret house where Hannibal was waiting for her. Now everything was in place for Dr. Lecter, all he had to do was “kill” Abigail, and implant her DNA residues on Will and wait for Will to come to Garret house thinking he will find Abigail. That way Hannibal was able to create plausible cause  for Will to have killed Abigail because of his condition and his strong connection to her that he had established from the first episode. After the evidence had been planted, Will is now wanted by the FBI. With no other place to go he arrives at Hannibal’s house and in a desperate attempt to connect the situation he asks Hannibal to go to Hobbs house and recreate the scene from the beginning of Hannibal’s copycat case. After that Will finally connects everything together and knows that Hannibal was behind everything that happened, that realization pushed him to try and kill Hannibal but at this moment Jack arrived, watching Will trying to kill Hannibal and Jack immobilizing Will before he supposedly takes another life, which Hannibal had anticipated. At the end , everyone assumes that Will’s personality disorder has taken over him, since Will first tried to use his ability to empathize with Hobbs, he became so invested with the case that without knowing it he killed a girl just to better understand him. And that was only the beginning. With Jack taking Will and using him to solve more cases he basically pushed Will even further into madness. Jack and everyone else thought that in every case Will was forced to use his disorder, he was also unintentionally becoming the killer. Basically, every murder in every case that was different from the original (and the FBI thought it was the work of a Copycat) was committed by Will in an attempt to better understand the killer, with the final victim of course being Abigail (aka the one from Will’s first case for the FBI). Thus Will gets arrested and is convicted for being the so-called Copycat killer. Hannibal was also able to anticipate that Will would deduce that he is the ripper since it was one of his goals, i will explain later why and he left a lot of clues for Will to connect. Hannibal had anticipated that when Will saw him, he would be able to deduce that Hannibal was the Chesapeake ripper. In context, Will becomes increasingly unstable, he starts investigating again from the beginning and has taken Abigail with him. When Will sees Hannibal in the Garret Jacob Hobbs house, he connects the dots, P1 : The crime scene of a copycat helped me to understand who Hobbs was. P2 : this killer has Psychological / medicine knowledge for the crime scene and Psychological profile. P3 : a lot of people could do that, Chilton, alana, Hannibal... Note : the killer who tried to kill Hannibal in S2 too and there are probably a lot of other people, people who could hide their Competences as well. P4 : The crime scene happened a bit after Hannibal was introduced to me. P5 : Hannibal didn't have a lot of time to create an accurate Psychological profile, compared to other potential suspects. P6 : though he had access to the files case P7 : He was with me when we discovered that Hobbs was the killer. P8 : He dropped some files and was alone for a few seconds. C : Hannibal called hobbs P9 : Hannibal is smart and knew that if he called hobbs, he would kill his family. P10 : Hannibal seems to be a manipulative person with Abigail, alana and his surrounding in general. C : Hannibal manipulation behavior suggests that he isn't a good person. P11 : Hannibal tried to understand me. P12 : he tried to isolate me from the FBI by manipulating me without me seeing it. P13 : Hannibal was aware of my illness and when I was with the doctor, he died. P14 : I was the last one who saw Marissa schurr. P15 : He tried to make me believe that I was the killer, while I thought that it was due to my empathy and dark self. P16 : He made me re-start the investigation from the beginning. P17 : He made me pass the test, like the clock. P18 : Hannibal visited my house. P19 : I'm the only one with some other people like Hannibal, Chilton who can understand criminals at such lvl. P20 : the killer created multiples Psychological profiles P21 : he did it to misdirect the FBI from him and to try to frame someone else P22 : Chilton fit the sociopath profile while no one really fit the other one and Hannibal does not fit any of these profile P23 : The killer might want to frame Hannibal as the killer. P24 : Hannibal is a genius, especially in terms of Psychology, confirmed by his insight P25 : Hannibal has everything to be the copycat. P26  : Even if the Killer created multiple different profiles to probably frame someone else, due to my fragile nature and problem, Hannibal showed his manipulative nature in front of me which suggests that he is not the one which is getting misdirected by another. P27 : Now Hannibal is there, just as if he planned everything. He re-created the Hobbs crime scene and I am Hoobs. Final C : Hannibal is the killer and tries to frame me.
You need to keep in mind that Hannibal knew due to Will's condition that he would only deduce this when he will see Hannibal in hobbs house again, like why is he even there? Hannibal strategically used Will's condition but he knew that at this moment Will would connect the dots especially because Hannibal is here while Abigail disappeared, also keep in mind that if Hannibal was the detective he could do the abduction as he scale narratively far above Will with statement of S3 and Will always ask Hannibal help for the case. Hannibal was able to anticipate this thought process and he created his frame in a way that Will could deduce that. Why did Hannibal let Will deduce that he is the Chesapeake ripper ? In the last episode, Will arrives at the truth and correctly describes Hannibal to the letter - and you can see how this is one of the greatest moments of Hannibal's life. The awe on his face and his reverent tone, especially when he tells Will he's unique and that he should cultivate his urges he's kept down for so long, telegraph this. Will knows he doesn't get into the minds of the serial killers he profiles and act out their murders. He knows what kind of killer he is - a righteous one, a killer who kills other killers. And Hannibal offers himself as a sacrifice - in Will's eyes, he killed Abigail, meaning he's fair prey. Hannibal doesn't try to talk Will down, doesn't try to take away his gun. At this point, he is willing to die if it gets Will to embrace his true self and his killer instincts. Hannibal had that smile by the end of Savoureux because the scales had finally fallen off from Will's eyes and Hannibal had revealed himself to Will. And also, because he knows all of his attempts to get Will to accept he's a killer nearly succeeded in the kitchen when he raised his gun at Hannibal, and was only stopped because they were interrupted by Jack, which was anticipated by Hannibal. Hannibal wanted Will to understand what type of Killer he was. Hannibal is curious to see how Will's mind works. He wants to see if Will has the capacity to pierce the truth of things and thus, see Hannibal for who he is. He also usually rewards Will with a piece of insight when Will isn't sidetracked by Hannibal's suggestions (which I've bolded in the following exchanges. Hannibal also framed Will Because he wanted Will to see him. Framing Will for all the CopyCat murders forces Will to reconstruct the Copy Cat's way of thinking, which leads to him understanding Hannibal and the way he thinks. Hannibal killed Marissa the same way he killed Cassie because Will said he wouldn't kill that way again. A test for Will, to see if he can understand the unpredictable way the Copy Cat thinks. If Will is in prison, Hannibal is still safe, for the time being. What's even more impressive is that Will thought he killed Abigail and ate her ears while it was Hannibal who created this, how? Well, they found her ears on Will's house but more importantly Will was convinced of being the killer seeing this and it even made more sense considering he was thinking exactly like hobbs. Will is now in jail and thinks he killed Abigail. in the dark of the jail he has time to think about the thought process of Hannibal, which makes him understand his mind, how he accepts himself, how he thinks, why Hannibal did this. It gives him time to think and to accept who he is, why he did it. Hannibal lived this too, since his sister died and Will loved Abigail like how Hannibal loved his sister, Hannibal knows that this is a necessary step.
The framing of Will is composed of many different strategies and elements that were all connected to achieve a specific goal. The framing of Will is comprised of: 1) Will’s psychological background itself (being able to think like a killer because of his disorder) 2) Will’s connection with Hobbs from Ep1 3) The strategy to distance Will from the FBI (by antagonising Jack and using Will’s personality disorder against him) 4) The creation of multiple murders (at least 5), each different from the other, with the difference in the original murders implying the existence of another killer who is present in all 5) The manipulation of Abigail from Ep3, to use her against Will 6) The Physical evidence at Will’s house 7) psychologically pressuring Will by indirectly putting him in dangerous situations to worsen his condition (Tobias, Gideon and Hobbs incidents) 8)  Manipulating Jack as the Chesapeake Ripper to use Will in more cases in order to worsen his mental state 9) The manipulation of Jack about Will’s condition (how Will’s condition makes him vonerable to the killers thoughs) 10) The gaslighting of Will himself about his psychological health 11) The usage of Abigail to isolate Will from the FBI by forcing him to hide Abigail’s murder 12) The concealment and fabrication of Will’s neurological problem (by faking the results of his test) 13) The Clock strategy (which was used to create the possibility of Will faking his own condition) 14) The memory losses strategy (destroying Will’s alibi, while also making Will himself believe Hannibal’s theory) 15) The recordings of Will’s and Hannibal’s conversations from the very beginning of the series 16)  The common factor in all the murders being Will 17) The ear of Abigail and the connection of Will to Hobbs 18) The manipulation of Will at the end to force him attack Hannibal and make him look like a killer.
It’s also worth noting that Will himself, after his framing, thought that this was all easy for Hannibal. Meaning that Hannibal wasn’t even trying his best to do all that.

Season 2

S2 Ep 1

- The episode begins with a fight between Jack and Hannibal set in the future, with Hannibal seemingly winning the fight. Then it's back to the present, Hannibal and Jack are both having dinner, there's going to be a trial of Will Graham at the FBI, and Hannibal has to be present.

- there will be an FBI investigation because people like Jack and Alana knew Will was up to no good and didn't stop him from going on another murder spree, "but he was saving people" Jack's excuse.

- Will has Hannibal's voice in his head, he can't get it out and it's probably because of the seizures he was having, the only thing he could hear was his voice.

- The FBI takes Hannibal's DNA and keeps his jacket for analysis, but obviously they'll never trace it back to Hannibal.

- Alana Bloom tries to get Will's memory back but doesn't succeed; Will visualizes a scene in which he's having dinner with Hannibal and starts to have a seizure. A little later, Will also imagines the moment when Hannibal puts Abigail's ear in his mouth, and gradually recovers his memory.

- At the dinner party between Chilton and Hannibal, Chilton tells Hannibal about this and that Will tried to cheat on Alana. At this point, Chilton is convinced that Will is indeed the Chesapeake Ripper, and he has absolutely no fear of Hannibal. He also tells him that Will tried to accuse Hannibal, he is obsessed with him, he also says to everyone that he is a monster.

- There have been new murders, which we'll show to Will, and he'll reply that the killer is composing a color palette, since each body has a different color. Hannibal also investigated the scene and said “I investigated like how Will will do it” meaning that he also should have understood that the killer is trying to create a palette of color.  (psychological insight, inductive reasoning)

- Jack is not convinced that Will didn’t know what he was doing compared to alana who thinks he didn’t know, her argument is that he is too afraid of the truth but he still tries to find it, while a psychopath wouldn't be afraid. Alana also states that Will is more focused on proving Hannibal guilt than his own defense.
Jack and Will will talk about the investigation on the claim Will made, Jack will state that they found nothing on Hannibal, they took his DNA, Fingerprint, there is nothing. Hannibal has left no evidence, even will does not have any evidence of what Hannibal did to him.

S2 Ep 2

- The killer from EP 1 has kidnapped many people and locked them up, one person escaped but the killer was there and killed him, the body was found. Hannibal replies that skin color is often a political issue, adding that the killer probably only sees the skin color of his victims, which is why he's able to inflict such things. The killer is likely to add new bodies to his color palette, so fibers, debris and cracks may indicate where they've been, Hannibal also talk about the craquelure which show that he knows a bit of french and knowing his intellect he probably master the languages (psychological insight, deductive & inductive reasoning, VCI)

- Bedelia goes to visit Hannibal to say that their session is over, with all the action that happened with Will graham and their old interaction especially with his attack she started to doubt Hannibal. Hannibal asks if she's spoken to Jack about it and she replies that she hasn't, that it's over and that Hannibal is dangerous and she doesn't want to see him anymore. In truth, it's obvious, but she's completely overwhelmed by fear; she's the only person at the moment with Will who's managed to see a little of Hannibal, and she knows just how dangerous he can be.

- Jack and bedelia are going to talk about Hannibal, except that bedelia doesn't want to be involved in the FBI story anymore, she doesn't want to be involved in anything. She wants to try to distance herself from Hannibal, and Jack tells her that Hannibal might try to help her with her problems, but of course she refuses.

- The police will show the photos to Will, who will conclude from the body that he survived what the killer did to him and managed to escape, but since he was already too injured, he ran and fell down a hill trying to escape. Will will also say that the killer needs an isolated location, like a farm close to places with a lake. Obviously, if the killer is kidnapping and torturing several people, he's not going to do it near people.

- Now we know that Hannibal understands this too, as he literally goes to the killer location. He has the same thought process as Will: the killer must be in an isolated place, probably a farm, given what was found on the body, and close to where it was found, given how badly the person was wounded. But he also smelled the corpse and he induced his location via the seeds that were growing there and he even visualized the place (inductive reasoning, insight, VSI)

- They find the scene and show it to Will, who visualizes the scene and says that the killer fixed the bodies at the place where it lies, this is my drawing. The dead eye of vision and conscience, I have the stare lost from the wave unless someone else sees me, one of these creatures is out of place, at moment he saw a person in the middle who's not in the right place on the killer's drawing, and then when he puts himself in that person's place and looks up into the sky, he sees the chesapeake Ripper.. this is exactly the situation that happened when the killer saw Hannibal. This person, who shouldn't be in his place and is in fact the killer, Hannibal drugged him and put him in his drawing to "finish him off". Why did he do that ?

Initially, Hannibal becomes aware of Beverly Katz's involvement when she starts seeking Will's insights on cases. This would naturally place her on his radar as a potential obstacle to his grand scheme. He foresaw that Will would encourage her to delve deeper into the mysteries surrounding him and that she needs to tell Jack all of this. This level of prediction aligns well with his character.

Hannibal's decision to save Jack's wife serves a dual purpose: it not only ingratiates him further into Jack's good graces but also ensures that Jack would be occupied and unavailable for consultation when Beverly will try to see him to tell him about what WIll said to her. This is a genius stroke that preemptively cuts off one of Beverly's most straightforward routes to corroborate her suspicions. With Jack out of the picture, it becomes increasingly likely for Beverly to take matters into her own hands and seek out evidence herself. Beverly will go to Hannibal home and find interesting proof in the basement but Beverly is caught in his basement, Hannibal's demeanor suggests he had prepared for the eventuality of an uninvited investigation.

- Also the coin toss that Hannibal made to decide the fate of Jack's wife, even if Hannibal had left her to die, Jack would still have been busy and grateful to Lecter for bringing his wife a little wellbeing before he died. The coin toss is just for Hannibal's amusement.

Sum of the strategy, Hannibal was basically able to plan the the moment when jack's wife wanted to commit suicide in the same day he gave big clues to beverly (that he created himself of course) who went to see Will, who told her to tell jack everything, but he was busy, so she went to Hannibal and was Hannibal was there to kill her. Chilton profile

Before starting to read Hannibal planning, we need to understand why Hannibal framed Chilton and how he made the Chesapeake ripper profile fit Chilton.
At the core of Chilton's character is a pervasive narcissistic personality disorder (NPD), a real and serious psychological condition characterized by extreme self-appraisal and self-judgment. Individuals with NPD are ambitious, determined, and constantly seek approval from others to define their self-worth. Chilton's behavior aligns with this definition: he shows little empathy, fails to form meaningful personal relationships, and has an inflated view of himself that drives him to pursue unorthodox psychiatric techniques and sensationalized achievements to maintain his self-esteem. His actions, from testifying at Will Graham's trial to his fateful Tattler interview about the Tooth Fairy, are fueled by a pathological need for recognition and validation, making him similar to Freddy Lounds from "Red Dragon," who also shifted from "straight journalism" to sensationalism to gain the attention he craved. Chilton likely began his career in traditional psychiatry, but finding it impossible to stand out, he turned to more controversial methods, eventually embracing pop-psychology for its accessibility and sensational appeal. Despite his intelligence and competence, Chilton's constant comparison to the best in his field often makes him appear foolish. His pursuit of fame and recognition becomes a kind of addiction, one that persists even after severe physical trauma. This unrelenting ambition suggests a deep-seated pathology rather than mere vanity, highlighting a troubling aspect of his character. Chilton’s background likely involves excessive praise from his mother and neglect from his father, a dynamic that fostered his narcissism. His mother's fading beauty and career contrast with his father's indifference, creating a void that Chilton attempts to fill through his professional achievements. His father’s neglect instilled in him a belief that he is inherently flawed, driving him to continuously seek approval. This need for validation is so profound that it overrides any concern for personal safety or quality of life, indicating a child-like mindset where merely being alive, regardless of circumstances, is enough. Elan Golomb's theory on NPD posits that such individuals feel deeply flawed and attempt to control how others perceive them to avoid rejection. Chilton's grandiosity and self-aggrandizement, therefore, are defenses against this perceived inadequacy. His relentless pursuit of recognition, despite repeated failures and humiliations, underscores his deep psychological need to prove his worth and mask his inner void. This interpretation casts Chilton's manipulative and arrogant behavior in a more somber light, suggesting that his flaws are rooted in a disorder that compels him to constantly seek validation. While it is easy to laugh at his pompousness or scorn his entitlement, understanding Chilton through the lens of NPD evokes a more nuanced response. His actions, driven by an unquenchable need for approval, reveal a tragic aspect of his character that blends both pitiful and reprehensible traits, challenging the audience to reconsider their perceptions of him. The symbolic power dynamics of cannibalism further reinforce Hannibal's sense of dominance and control, as he consumes his victims to assert his superiority. Hannibal's surgical precision and medical knowledge add another layer of complexity to the Ripper's profile. Hannibal fits the profile for the Ripper, and Chilton notably both reveres and envies him. All this could be seen as Chilton setting Gideon up to be his patsy and when that failed, setting up Dr Lecter instead. Chilton had terrible surgical skills! Yeah, and he kept that a secret because he didn't want to risk his professional status. He even says he's a "doctor of note" to Will. He could even pretend to not be skilled as part of his person suit to avert suspicion. Chilton lost a kidney and had trouble digesting protein. There was no proof the Ripper was eating his victims, it was just a theory put forward by Will and this was used by Hannibal to make it looks like Chilton was innocenting himself because he couldn't eat, even though before this event Chilton could and it make sense with his narcissistic personality because the Chesapeake Ripper ate his victim because he thought that he was superior on the other hand Hannibal is stated to be able to control his emotions perfectly, even though he think that he is superior to anyone he never show it which added another layers of shielding for him.

The Framing of Chilton

Hannibal wanted to misdirect the FBI and to do it he had one way, Frame Chilton. Firstly, why Chilton ? Chilton took part in the Chesapeake Ripper investigation before Will, Hannibal Lecter and Miriam lass so he had access to the case file, he had all the information he wanted. Chilton is an expert in medicine and psychiatry, which means he is very intelligent. Chilton was accused multiple times of psychologically driving his patients + using unorthodox methods. He was in charge of Gideon who was accused of being the Chesapeake Ripper. Chilton doesn't feel any remorse for his actions, doesn't take responsibility for them, doesn't take steps to be better either, he is kind of sociopath and egoistic man which fit their first psychological profile of the chesapeake ripper while Hannibal doesn’t explained as above in Chilton profile.

- Now as Hannibal wanted to misdirect the FBI, he had to misdirect Chilton in a perfect way. His first way to incriminate Chilton was using Miriam lass, he started manipulating her, he mentally manipulated her to make her react to chilton voice, when she will hear his voice she Will say that he is the chesapeake Ripper while if she hear Hannibal voice she Will innocent him, he also manipulated her to remember that the last thing she saw was the book “wounded man”. To do this, Hannibal need time and with only this he won’t Incriminate Chilton perfectly, so he waited some time, while he was waiting he was trying to manipulate Miriam and he also heard the rumors about Gideon being the chesapeake ripper, Gideon who is in charge of Chilton. Finally when he heard about Will graham, he started re killing people, because now that Will who has a high level of empathy started investigating the chesapeake ripper case, he can use him to frame Chilton . But why Will? Basically it must be due to his ability, you need to remember that many actions of Hannibal are for his fun. Framing someone like Will with an ability like that will be more fun than an average FBI investigator, also Hannibal must have heard rumors about Will intelligence and profiling, framing Will and not an average FBI investigator means that after Will will be framed, no one smarter than him could come to investigate the case or no one will disturb him after, when he will try to frame Chilton. Hannibal sees in Will someone like him, he also framed him because he wanted Will to embrace this darker half, the whole plot of the season 1 is that Will had not done something horrible in killing Garrett Jacob Hobbs. It was that he had enjoyed it as much as he did, so much so that he all but repeated the same activity within a week or so when he shot Eldon Stammets. He was only saved by his poor aim, not his lack of desire to see the act through. Like Peter Bernardone, Will used to have a terrible fear of hurting anybody, but Hannibal helped him “get over that.” Again, this was all laid out in Season 1–there would have been no plot in Season 1 if Will hadn’t feared that he was sliding down a very slippery slope in regards to his own ability to embrace murder. They hit that point with him again and again and again, with Freddie Lounds’s words about him, the obvious pleasure he got out of imagining some crime scenes, his dreams and hallucinations, and his general willingness to shoot people–including Hannibal in the Season 1 finale. Also as Will got a strange ability, Hannibal must have thought that if he frame Will he might end up in the Chilton Psychiatric Hospital, if he end up there he just need to make WIll hate him so that he try to use what he in the hospital to attack Hannibal and what he has? He has Chilton, If Hannibal is attacked by Will, the police will obviously make the connection to Chilton. Hannibal started killing again, he created several psychological profiles to create a first layer of misdirection, if by chance he sees through it he'll understand that there's only one killer, and then he'll come to the conclusion that Will is the killer, since Hannibal has been gathering evidence against Will all along. In the series' scenario, it happened like that but much more slowly, as Hannibal had plenty of time to set up the murders and connect them against Will. All these events were orchestrated by Hannibal, here the strategy. Hannibal’s Strategy To Use The Vergers: What happened between Will and Margot was a part of Hannibal’s strategy to isolate and psychologically push Will towards killing. This strategy involves using the Vergers to give Will the opportunity of connection and family while at the same time taking it away from him. Hannibal started this by isolating Will from everyone he was close with. He seduced Alana (the one person Will was in love with) and turned her against Will. By doing this, he made Will desperate for connection. At the same time Hannibal was manipulating Margot. His manipulation involved Hannibal pushing Margot to kill her brother so that he could better understand the situation of the siblings. When he learned that Margot couldn’t kill her brother because she would lose everything he then offered her a way out by having an heir. At the same time Hannibal made sure that she and Will were gonna meet by making their appointment next to each other (indirectly creating the idea of a successor in Will). After that Hannibal had set the stage for everything. Will’s loneliness, along with Margot’s need for a successor, made the 2 come into contact. After that there was no deceiving. Will wanted Margot to feel the connection he had with Alana in the past and Margot wanted Will for a successor (they were both using one another. Both for their own reasons). Margot didn’t manipulate Will more than Will manipulated Margot. But at the end, they were both manipulated by Hannibal. Hannibal was behind everything and Will realized it. After that he indirectly gave Will the idea of family and specifically, the feeling of fatherhood (something that Will had with Abigail). Making him feel those emotions again he basically made Will a new person. Hannibal had given Will the one thing that he even felt close to, which is his relationship with Abigail. This isn’t even the first time that Hannibal was able to understand Will’s relationship with Abigail and had used it to his advantage. Will’s feelings for Abigail were very strong because throughout Will’s  investigation of Hobbs’s murders (from Ep1), Will was continually using his personality disorder to empathize with him and better understand him. This led to Will imitating the emotional state of Hobbs along with his feelings. One of those feelings was his love for his daughter and thus Will feels a special connection with Abigail (and even a heavy sense of responsibility about her). After that Hannibal manipulated Mason into thinking that his sister was planning to have an heir and later kill him. That of course led to Mason capturing his sister and performing a surgery to kill the unborn child and making her unable to ever have children again (essentially taking Will fatherhood away). After that Hannibal expected Will to just kill Mason. Will wanted to kill him, more than anything else. After all this time Will finally had a chance to feel the same way he felt about Abigail, and Mason had taken it away. But Will realized what happened. He realized that this was all Hannibal’s plan. So he didn’t kill Mason as much as that hurt him. That was all Hannibal’s strategy to push Will into killing Mason.
(Strategy Efficiency - Foresight, STP, EU, Anticipation, Indirect Manipulation)

S2 Ep 12

- Now let’s explain the complexity of the situation. I said that Will hasn’t forgiven Hannibal and that Hannibal knows it, he is trying to encourage this thought but he also knows that Will doesn’t actually want to kill him. Will failed to shoot him in his kitchen, and Hannibal takes full advantage of Will insisting that there are no more lies between them to make sure that Will isn’t going to try to kill him again. The point of that conversation about lying is so that we and Hannibal both understand that whatever Will does from that point on out is in the spirit of honesty, even though we know, Hannibal does not, that Will has resumed his therapy in order to present himself to Hannibal as lure. We continue to see Will’s affection for Hannibal grow. It’s evident in their shared conversations and eventually culminates in Will’s choice to save Hannibal from the pigs–which Hannibal was witness to also, of course. We finally see Will’s worry for Hannibal etched plainly in his face, when he asks Hannibal if he could be happy while incarcerated by living in his mind palace. Even Bedelia du Maurier notices it from afar, when she asks Jack Crawford, “Do you still think he is your killer?” and Jack also comments on it when he tells Will not to “let empathy confuse what you want with what Lecter wants.” in Season 1: his empathy makes Hannibal’s feelings for him profoundly real to him. He knows just how much Hannibal loves him–as no other person has probably ever loved him in his memory, and indeed Will has been thinking about little else besides Hannibal for months. Hannibal saw in Will humanity, but he also saw in him someone who couldn't control his emotions and accept his true self, the complete inverse of him. Hannibal isn’t embarrassed to cry in the opera or to be the first to stand up and applaud; he delights in stereotypically ‘feminine’ hobbies like cooking and clothes selection; he draws fan-art and openly expresses his admiration. When Will buys a gift for Abigail in the same E4 and freaks out, Hannibal asks him: Hannibal: Feeling paternal, Will? Will’s reaction is instant and defensive: Will: Aren’t you? Hannibal easily says “yes”, which disarms Will. This is a great contrast between them: Hannibal isn’t afraid to talk and acknowledge his feelings while Will is embarrassed of them and shies away from them. In fact, this is a repeat of their conversation in E2. One other thing Season 1 demonstrated was just how muddied with another person’s identity Will can get when he takes on their thoughts and behaviors in the extremely unique manner he has of profiling them. Will cleans up his appearance both for Hannibal’s sake and to get into Hannibal’s mindset; he starts cooking gourmet meals for his dogs; even before he was released from the mental hospital, he used his powers of perception to manipulate those around him, just as Hannibal would do. In empathizing with Hannibal, he sees things from Hannibal’s point of view. It should come as no surprise, then, that the path to his reckoning becomes less clear by the day. Hannibal knows how Will’s mind works, probably better than we do. As Will explores both his dark side and his attachment to Hannibal, Hannibal anticipates that the way Will thinks is changing. And it is–Will says he never felt as alive as he did when he was killing Randall Tier. He’s not troubled by guilt after he kills Randall and displays him, and he doesn’t mind at all what happened to Mason Verger, though he’s a little indignant that Hannibal involved his dogs. This is why the deception works: Hannibal doesn't need to believe that Will has forgiven him in order to believe Will’s deception. He only needs to believe that Will is in the process of changing into his more violent self, a version of him who could kill Freddie Lounds. He needs to believe that Will has become so entangled in Hannibal’s manner of thinking, that his own manner of thinking has changed, enough to do things that the law would never allow. He needs to believe that Will has given up on the law–and this is not a far leap for Will, who was incarcerated for crimes he didn’t commit, who knew that Margot Verger would never see any legal justice for the acts of her brother (which cost Will another child), who was frustrated with the travesty of justice of a “vulgar” legal system more based in “advertising” than truth, and who told Alana Bloom that “Faith in any sort of legal justice has never been any more comforting than a nightlight.” Will has been ready to kill Hannibal three times before, and twice would have gone through with it if Jack hadn’t stopped him. Add to that what Will does with Randall Tier and then apparently does to Freddie Lounds, and Hannibal has a huge amount of accurate evidence that Will has lost interest working within the bounds of the law. Finally, what Hannibal needs in order to believe that Will might come with him is the simple fact that the law might well be coming after Will. Will doesn’t have to be entirely happy with the thought of running away with Hannibal. He just has to see it as a necessity and be willing to go along with it because the alternative would be incarceration for the deaths of Randall Tier, Freddie Lounds, and even the attack on Mason Verger, and this time with no one to conveniently let him out again. Hannibal knows that he’s going to need to continue leaning on Will’s weak points to continue to condition him to murder, and he knows that a part of Will is still angry and still finds pleasure in hurting Hannibal, as evidenced by Will putting the “snare” around Hannibal’s neck with Mason Verger. From Hannibal’s perspective, both of these things are minor inconveniences at worst and delightful experiments at best. Neither require forgiveness and neither mean that Will would prevent himself from continuing to explore his fascinating relationship with Hannibal and with himself. Hannibal just needs to believe that Will is living in the moment. Forgiveness can come later, when time reverses and teacups come together. Hannibal had that part all figured out too. Ultimately, Hannibal falls for Will’s deception because it isn’t deception. Will really does care for Hannibal’s uniqueness as Hannibal cares for his, and as no one has cared for either man in their lives; he really does explore his murderous nature, which is so like Hannibal’s; he really does elicit the suspicion of both Alana Bloom and Jack Crawford. Hannibal believes in all of these truths about Will, just as Hannibal believes in his own ability to influence Will, Hannibal wants to trust Will, he lets Will see him and he desesperatly trusts him. Their union ought to be as inevitable as Hannibal imagines (emotional understanding, direct & indirect manipulation, psychoanalysis, inducement, deception, abstract reasoning)
