The Complete Summary of Hannibal’s Plan to Avoid The FBI:

Hannibal’s Layers and Strategies

Hannibal first started to operate as the Chesapeake Ripper almost 4.5 years before the start of the series. Before he even began his exploits he had already created a plan to escape the police if they ever came too close. This plan was to frame Dr Chilton of the Baltimore State Hospital as the Chesapeake Ripper (the profile that Hannibal would create for his future murders). Hannibal’s choice of Chilton wasn’t random, he picked him because of the similarities in the psychological backgrounds between him and Chilton, also because Chilton hates him and that The Chesapeake Ripper kills Rude people, Chilton is egoistic. Chilton doesn't feel any remorse for his actions, doesn't take responsibility for them, doesn't take steps to be better either, he is the kind of sociopath and egoistic man which fit their first psychological profile of the chesapeake ripper. Hannibal also fits some part of the profile for the Ripper, and Chilton notably both reveres and envies him. All this could be seen as Chilton setting Gideon up to be his patsy and when that failed, setting up Dr Lecter instead. Chilton had terrible surgical skills! Yeah, and he kept that a secret because he didn't want to risk his professional status. He even says he's a "doctor of note" to Will. He could even pretend to not be skilled as part of his person suit to avert suspicion. Chilton lost a kidney and had trouble digesting protein. There was no proof the Ripper was eating his victims, it was just a theory put forward by Will and this was used by Hannibal to make it looks like Chilton was innocenting himself because he couldn't eat, even though before this event Chilton could and it make sense with his narcissistic personality because the Chesapeake Ripper ate his victim because he thought that he was superior on the other hand Hannibal is stated to be able to control his emotions perfectly, even though he think that he is superior to anyone he never show it which added another layers of shielding for him. Hannibal wanted to kill people and take their organs to eat. By killing unworthy and rude people, in his eyes, Hannibal was using them to create something beautiful, which was his dishes. So the killer would require some kind of medical or surgical knowledge, which Dr Chilton had. So after he started to operate as the Ripper, the FBI got involved in the case as Hannibal was expecting and they asked Chilton to help them profile the killer based on his psychological knowledge and understanding (which made Hannibal’s plan better because of the strong connection Chilton had with the case). Hannibal killed his victims in sounders of three, he killed his first 3 victims in 9 days. Then he didn’t kill again for 18 months, he might have done that since the FBI contacted Chilton to profile the killer which means Chilton was indirectly monitored by the FBI and this is why it would be harder to kill. Then after 18 months he killed another sounder of three in 9 days, he probably did that since after 18 months Chilton would probably not be trying to profile the killer and therefore be with the FBI. The word “sounder” refers to a small group of pigs, and that’s how Hannibal sees his victims, not as people, but as pigs. 11 months after the 6th victim, the Ripper killed his 7th victim 2 days later (again the same pattern, Hannibal killed then stopped in order to deceive the FBI into thinking “when chilton investigates there is no killing”)., his 8th victim was found dead in his workshop. Hannibal used every tool in the workshop to piece the victim and as with other victims organs were removed. The mutilation and removal of organs means someone with surgical or anatomical knowledge. Believing that, an FBI agent, Miriam Lass was investigating all the known medical records in the area until she disappeared, she’s believed to be the Ripper’s 9th victim but no traces of her were found, until 2 years after her disappearance. In the middle of her investigation she found a connection between Hannibal and one of the victims of the ripper case so she went to interview Hannibal without the permission of Jack (because of the confidentiality law). It was at this moment that Hannibal was able to knock her out and take her alive. In all of this time Hannibal didn’t leave even a shred of evidence and was able to avoid the police. As Will said “Throughout the year, the Chesapeake Ripper has remained entirely and consistently theatrical” (S1 E7). But after the disappearance of Miriam, Hannibal suddenly stopped his killings as the Ripper. A short while after the disappearance Gideon, a transplant surgeon with the same psychological background as the Chesapeake Ripper, was also admitted into the Baltimore State Hospital For The Criminally Insane in which Chilton, was in charge of. Hannibal intentionally remained silent and off the radar for 2 years, creating the idea that the Chesapeake ripper might have been captured without the FBI knowing (pointing to Gideon) or that he might have disappeared. Hannibal was planning to use Gideon and his connection to Chilton and the Ripper case in the future. So Hannibal remained quiet until he met Will in one of the FBI’s cases about Hobbs aka the Minnesota Shrike. Hannibal was very intrigued by Will's condition that he was able to diagnose immediately and wanted to help Will catch the killer while also creating another plan in his mind to frame him. Why Will? Well, because Hannibal was able to completely understand Will's psychological condition which was his ability to get into the mind of criminals aka his personality disorder. That condition also made Will a potential threat because of his overwhelming empathy and Hannibal knew it. So he decided from the moment he met Will that he was going to frame him for his upcoming murders (which each of them would be entirely different and unrelated with each other). But not only that, Hannibal was also planning to use Will in the future (after his frame) as a potential evidence against Chilton (his 3rd layer) and also framed him to make him accept his true self.  Hannibal committed multiple murders in the next month and he was able to create a different psychological profile for each one of them. Some of them were discussed as the work of a copycat (Hobbs’s copycat) and others even like the work of organ harvesters, without a single evidence left behind. Hannibal wanted all of those murders to be close to the original but not too close. He wanted the FBI to think that this might be the work of a copycat so that if they were ever able to connect them all together they would find the person who was present in all of them (aka Will Graham). Basically Hannibal was able to create enough connections just to make the FBI even question the existence of another killer. He did that by creating connections between the murders that were very difficult to find (like the fact that all of them were not exactly the same as the first one or that some of them were only connected via Will ( like the death of the Doctor and the girl in S1 E10)). He created those seemingly impossible connections so that even if the FBI is somehow capable of finding them, they would all be connected to Will and not Hannibal. It also helped that after his meeting with Hannibal, Jack was so impressed with his insights that he offered to become Will’s therapist, which gave him the chance to stay close to the case and to Will. By having that information about the different cases the FBI was working on, Hannibal was able to create unreliable murders with seemingly no connection to one another, making his exploits very difficult for the FBI to connect. [First Layer of Deception]

———————————————————————————
So Hannibal was protected under those seemingly random killings while also manipulating Will and strategically creating his framing in case the FBI was able to connect all of those killings into a single killer. This happened at the end of Season one, when Will was able to connect all of the previous killings to a single killer he named the Copycat, but it was too late as Hannibal had already prepared the saga for Will’s framing. Hannibal’s strategy to perfectly frame Will was the following: It all started in the first episode of the series, when Hannibal met Will for the first time. Hannibal was able to immediately understand Will’s condition and empathize with him. It was at their first meeting that Hannibal decided that Will was the perfect person to use as an escape route if the FBI came too close to him, because of his rare condition (being someone who could literally think like a killer because of his empathy). After their first meeting Hannibal decided to help Will catch Hobbs by creating a crime scene that would make Will indirectly look into the mind of Hobbs. After he helped Will to locate Hobbs he warned Hobbs about the situation because he knew how he would react by perfectly understanding him. He intentionally put Will in a position where he was forced to kill Hobbs in order to save Abigail. Hannibal wanted Will to empathize with Hobbs in a great way for 3 reasons: 1) Hannibal knew how Hobbs would react and he wanted to show to the world that Will was capable of murder 2) He wanted to create a strong connection between Abigail and Will via Hobbs so that he could use her to frame Will in the future. Hannibal wanted to establish that Will was forming the same relationship that Hobbs had with his daughter. A relationship that ended with Hobbs trying to kill Abigail 3) He wanted Will to empathize with Hobbs so much, to make Will feel like he was the one who killed those girls that Hobbs had killed. That way, Hannibal could make Will confess those feelings about the case in one of their sessions and use them as evidence for Will’s framing in the future. This first incident was just the beginning of Hannibal’s strategy to frame Will. By indirectly leading Will into the killer, Hannibal expected that Hobbs would try and kill his family before they captured him, so that Will would be forced to kill Hobbs and show to the world that Will is capable of violence in the first place. In every murder Hannibal committed he was leaving space for the existence of another killer that the original (a copycat) by leaving some subtle differences between the original murder and the one Hannibal was committing. This difference was made to point out to the existence of a single killer that was present in all of those murders (Will Graham) so that even if the FBI is able to connect all of those seemingly unrelated murders, they would find Will and not Hannibal as the killer. Also, from very early on, Hannibal was trying to antagonize Jack to Will in order to make Will distance himself from everyone, allowing Hannibal’s future strategies to be more effective. After the incident with Hobbs, Hannibal also started visiting Will’s house when Will was away and planted physical evide of his crimes in the most hidden places. For Hannibal’s framing of Will to be more successful he needed Abigail. So Hannibal created a strategy to force Abigail into killing a man and then psychologically manipulate her into believing that this was her fault, while also helping her dispose of the body. By doing that, Hannibal forced Abigail to depend on him emotionally, so that he could further use her to isolate Will from the FBI. This happened by manipulating Will and understanding his feelings about Abigail. Hannibal also decided to share crucial information about Abigail’s murder with him, knowing that Will would be forced to keep it in order to save her life and by extension, forcing Will to come closer to Hannibal by cutting him off from the rest of his colleagues. By forcing Will to lie in order to save Abigail’s life he also made Will emotionally dependent on him by putting him in a similar situation. That’s why Hannibal could continue to manipulate Will in the future. While Hannibal was trying to isolate Will from the rest of the team, he was also trying to create the image that Will was slowly losing his mind because of his personality disorder (he achieved that by making Will clash with Jack and later by using Will’s condition). Hannibal was able to successfully create the idea in everyone that Will was losing his mind when he was at a crime scene in two ways: 1) As Will’s psychiatrist and an expert in the field, he was able to explain to Jack how Will's ability actually works. He explained that every time Will was at a crime scene he wasn’t only able to reflect the thoughts of the killers but also to absorb them (using Will’s empathy disorder as an argument to make him look more dangerous) and 2) by strategically inducing some of Will’s symptoms like the memory losses by using repeating lights to create seizures, so that when Will was on a crime scene it would look like his theory about Will’s condition was right. The most crucial part of Hannibal’s strategy started after he deduced Will’s Encephalitis. Hannibal had smelled Will’s encephalitis from the very beginning, and was using indirect & direct psychological methods  to worsen it further. To do that, Hannibal was also manipulating Jack by psychologically pushing him as the Chesapeake Ripper (sending him messages of his dead trainee) in order to make him more invested in the case and by doing this, force Jack to use Will more frequently in his cases (worsening Will’s condition). This also created the idea that Jack was the one who pushed Will into losing his mind by forcing him to use his personality disorder in more cases. At times even Hannibal will send Will in dangerous situations (like when he sends Will after Tobias) to expose him to stress and worsen his condition. At the same time Hannibal was concealing Will’s neurological symptoms by hiding his special neglect from him and he was even fabricating the results of his neurological tests to make it look like Will’s condition wasn’t neurological but rather psychological. He did that by persuading the Doctor using his charisma to lie to Will about his condition and then killing the Doctor by making it look like another killer did it (this murder will also be used against Will in the future). As early as episode 4, Hannibal visits Will’s house and scopes out the best available objects in his house to plant the remnants of his victims later on. Will’s worsened mental state entailed spatial negligence, time loss and identity crisis. After he aroused these symptoms, he planned to use them as concrete and plausible evidence for his frame. He did that by 1)Purposefully exposes Will to psychologically stressful situations that naturally worsen his condition, ensuring he becomes more susceptible to manipulation. 2)By acting as Will’s psychiatrist, Hannibal gains the opportunity to steer him away from accurate diagnoses and effective treatments, allowing the symptoms to escalate unchecked, while also remaining in touch with the progress of the FBI’s investigation of his case. 3)Strategically inducing Will’s crises and memory losses by using a form of light therapy in order to gaslight Will and further manipulate him. 4)During their therapy sessions, Hannibal skillfully seeds thoughts and suggestions that contribute to Will's ongoing confusion, thereby exacerbating his condition in a non-medical but deeply impactful way. Hannibal was cultivating him, waiting for Will to talk about how uncertain he was about himself in their session, and how good it felt to kill. Hannibal was precisely waiting for this moment, and vaguely guiding Will into saying these words so that he would be able to record them and use it as evidence for Will’s incrimination later on, to further perfect this frame from every perspective, in order to not be suspected at all. As we know, Will’s time loss was quite significant and repetitive, he was losing a conscious awareness of time, and blacking out at random points, not knowing what nor remembering what he did whilst he was in the blackout state. Will’s memory losses were induced by Hannibal himself to achieve the following goals: 1) To reinforce the idea that Will’s condition was related with the crime scene and his work by controlling the timing of his memory losses. Will would lose his memory only when he was at the present of a crime or when he was trying to empathize with the killer (which Hannibal also used to make Will think that this was all a desperate reaction of his brain to avoid the pressure) making it look like Will was indeed absorbing the killer’s thoughts and feelings. 2) To destroy Will’s alibi at the time of his crisis. When Will lost time he was unable to recall anything, including his actions and whereabouts at the moment of the crisis. This not only made Will scared about his potential actions but also Jack and the FBI. Another way Hannibal was able to frame Will was by concealing his true condition, which was the encephalitis. Hannibal made Will draw a clock to supposedly help him with his psychological trauma, but in reality Hannibal wanted to use this clock to hide Will’s true condition from the rest of the world. Hannibal had already anticipated that Dr. Bloom would test Will for encephalitis, in order to assess his spatial negligence, by making him draw a clock. So, what Hannibal already had done, was sketch a normal clock, and presented it to the FBI as if Will had drawn it himself. He had anticipated that Alana would test Will for encephalitis, and make him sketch a clock, so he took advantage of this forethought, and drew a normal clock in advance, and cloaked it with the lie that Will drew it. These contradicting sketches were to induce and manipulate the law enforcement into thinking another possibility, which was: 1) Will is smart. What if he was faking his assessments, and deluded everyone into thinking he was struck by encephalitis, so that he would later on use it as his alibi for his crimes? This is what Hannibal’s clock brought. Made them consider the possibility, and worsen his confinement condition further. After all these were implemented, it was relatively easier for Hannibal to execute his strategy. He only had to plant physical evidence now. Will’s induced memory losses allow Hannibal to execute the final part of his strategy. Will takes Abigail with him back to the Hobbs’ house with the hope of making her retrieve her previous memories regarding that incident. Will wanted to re-start from the beginning to find new clues about the case, Will was extremely stressed, his mental state was at its worst, triggering a crisis during which Abigail took the opportunity to leave, heading for the Garret house where Hannibal was waiting for her. Now everything was in place for Dr. Lecter, all he had to do was “kill” Abigail, and implant her DNA residues on Will and wait for Will to come to Garret house thinking he will find Abigail. That way Hannibal was able to create plausible cause  for Will to have killed Abigail because of his condition and his strong connection to her that he had established from the first episode. After the evidence had been planted, Will is now wanted by the FBI. With no other place to go he arrives at Hannibal’s house and in a desperate attempt to connect the situation he asks Hannibal to go to Hobbs house and recreate the scene from the beginning of Hannibal’s copycat case. After that Will finally connects everything together and knows that Hannibal was behind everything that happened, that realization pushed him to try and kill Hannibal but at this moment Jack arrived, watching Will trying to kill Hannibal and Jack immobilizing Will before he supposedly takes another life, which Hannibal had anticipated. At the end , everyone assumes that Will’s personality disorder has taken over him, since Will first tried to use his ability to empathize with Hobbs, he became so invested with the case that without knowing it he killed a girl just to better understand him. And that was only the beginning. With Jack taking Will and using him to solve more cases he basically pushed Will even further into madness. Jack and everyone else thought that in every case Will was forced to use his disorder, he was also unintentionally becoming the killer. Basically, every murder in every case that was different from the original (and the FBI thought it was the work of a Copycat) was committed by Will in an attempt to better understand the killer, with the final victim of course being Abigail (aka the one from Will’s first case for the FBI). In the last episode, Will arrives at the truth, Hannibal was also able to anticipate that Will would deduce that he is the ripper since it was one of his goals, he left a lot of clues for Will to connect. Hannibal had anticipated that when Will saw him, he would be able to deduce that Hannibal was the Chesapeake ripper. In context, Will becomes increasingly unstable, he starts investigating again from the beginning and has taken Abigail with him. When Will sees Hannibal in the Garret Jacob Hobbs house, he connects the dots and must have thought "The killer is probably the one who called Garret Jacob hobbs that day, Hannibal knew who he was but he was with me, so he found a way to call him somehow. With Abigail, they hid Nicholas' body, Hannibal manipulated Abigail into doing this, to gain her trust and he had managed to misdirect the FBI on Nicholas, he even convinced me of helping him and Abigail in this case.. During all those sessions, he tried to make me lose my mind, to control me and to understand me more and more. When I was alone with the doctor to see how my encephalitis was evolving, the doctor died, and when that girl was alone, I was the last one to see her. Someone is trying to frame me, I've been in contact with Hobbs, marissa schurr, nicholas, the doctor. I'm the only person who can fully understand criminals like Hobbs or that could create so many different psychological profile and who could make these crime scene but I know someone else who might too... and that person is Hannibal Lecter, the one who tried to make me unstable, who tried to make me believe that I was the killer,  who tried to make me believe that i was like Garret Jacob Hobbs, that i was a killer. Hannibal isolated me from the FBI, he made me restart the investigation from the beginning by making me bring Abigail with him as if to recreate the scene from the beginning of our story" Hannibal was able to anticipate this deductive thought process. So when Will arrives at the truth you can see how this is one of the greatest moments of Hannibal's life. The awe on his face and his reverent tone, especially when he tells Will he's unique and that he should cultivate his urges he's kept down for so long, telegraph this. Will knows he doesn't get into the minds of the serial killers he profiles and act out their murders. He knows what kind of killer he is - a righteous one, a killer who kills other killers. And Hannibal offers himself as a sacrifice - in Will's eyes, he killed Abigail, meaning he's fair prey. Hannibal doesn't try to talk Will down, doesn't try to take away his gun. At this point, he is willing to die if it gets Will to embrace his true self and his killer instincts. Hannibal had that smile by the end of Savoureux because the scales had finally fallen off from Will's eyes and Hannibal had revealed himself to Will. And also, because he knows all of his attempts to get Will to accept he's a killer nearly succeeded in the kitchen when he raised his gun at Hannibal, and was only stopped because they were interrupted by Jack, which was anticipated by Hannibal. Hannibal wanted Will to understand what type of Killer he was. I also think Hannibal is curious to see how Will's mind works. He wants to see if Will has the capacity to pierce the truth of things and thus, see Hannibal for who he is. He also usually rewards Will with a piece of insight when Will isn't sidetracked by Hannibal's suggestions (which I've bolded in the following exchanges. Hannibal also framed Will Because he wanted Will to see him. Framing Will for all the CopyCat murders forces Will to reconstruct the Copy Cat's way of thinking, which leads to him understanding Hannibal and the way he thinks, Hannibal killed Marissa the same way he killed Cassie because Will said he wouldn't kill that way again. A test for Will, to see if he can understand the unpredictable way the Copy Cat thinks.  What's even more impressive is that Will thought he killed Abigail and ate her ears while it was Hannibal who created this, how? Well, they found her ears on Will house but more importantly Will was convinced of being the killer seeing this and it even made more sense considering he was thinking exactly like hobbs. Will is now in jail and thinks he killed Abigail. in the dark of the jail he has time to think about the thought process of Hannibal, which makes him understand his mind, how he accepts himself, how he thinks, why Hannibal did this. It gives him time to think and to accept who he is, why he did it. Hannibal lived this too, since his sister died and Will loved abigail like how Hannibal loved his sister, Hannibal know that this is a necessary step
Will gets arrested and is convicted for being the so-called Copycat killer.
[Second Layer of Deception]

————————————————-——————————
With Will now in prison Hannibal is clear from any suspicion,except Will’s. Will knows that Hannibal is the killer and the problem is that some of his friends like Beverly want to believe him. So Hannibal prepares the stage ones again for his final plan that he was preparing all of those years ago, to connect all of the previous murders along with the Copycat murders to Chilton. if they ever understand that someone framed Will, they will think he was framed him because the killer want to make them think that he is the chesapeake ripper while the real goal of Hannibal when he framed Will was to use him to frame Chilton, even if they understood that the real goal of the killer wasn’t to frame Will to innocent himself, they will never understand that Hannibal did that for Frame Chilton because he would just need to use miriam lass to accuse Chilton directly, this is one of the main reason Hannibal framed Will. Hannibal’s strategy starts with the trial of Will which is the first proof against Chilton. The first thing Hannibal needs to do is to create a strong connection between Will and his case and Dr Chilton. This comes easy as Hannibal could anticipate that after he frames Will he will go to the Baltimore State Hospital for the Criminally Insane which Chilton is in charge of. So the connection has already been established. At the trial of Will, Chilton begins by talking about the fact that Will Graham is an intelligent psychopath who tries to make people believe that he’s innocent. Chilton also argues that he would do a much better job at helping Will surpass his psychological condition than Jack and Hannibal who let all of this happen. The fact that Chilton is so focused on incriminating Will makes people wonder about an alternative motive for Chilton to want Will in his hospital. On the other hand, Hannibal is one of the only people who constantly tries to explain Will’s psychological condition and never incriminate him (creating an antithesis between himself (who is with Will) and Chilton who wants Will in his hospital). So this is the first proof against Chilton, why would Chilton accuse Will graham? That’s pretty obvious, if he was the chesapeake ripper he do that because he want Will to be framed (this is what Hannibal will try to make it looks) but in reality it’s because he is connected to Gideon who was accused of being the chesapeake ripper and there are also rumors about Chilton using unorthodox method, Chilton want to end all this case, that is how Hannibal predicted that he would to this. Chilton could possibly accuse Hannibal by saying Will condition worsened since he is his psychologist, or at least he could make it looks like this, which would put the FBI on Hannibal traces, but since Chilton will be framed and that there is multiples moments in which Chilton accuse Hannibal, the FBI would conclude that Chilton is behind this and that he manipulated Will to accuse Hannibal. One example of how Chilton indirectly accused Hannibal (but this time without even noticing it, Hannibal crafted this situation),Beverly was found dead after talking to Will about Hannibal, which would incriminates Hannibal because it seems like Hannibal killed her because she believe in will, but since the FBI will knows in the future  that chilton is the real killer, it just looks like Chilton tried to indirectly accuse Hannibal by killing her. Now the next part which was anticipated by Hannibal is Will asking a pact to Chilton to help him recover his memory and to ask Chilton to say that now Will relies entirely on Chilton. Chilton gives Will an injection with amorbital that causes him to fall into a seizure that reminds him of a day he spent with Hannibal in his office, where the light was flashing and he was giving him an injection. The light was in fact something often used in neurotherapy as a treatment, but in Will's case it was used to trigger his seizures that was used for his memory losses in the second layer. This will also be used later as a proof against Chilton, Plus, Hannibal had anticipated that 1) Will would try to manipulate Chilton 2) he would want to incriminate Hannibal, and 3) that he will recover his memories to have more information of how Hannibal had framed him. Chilton is also a bit scared of Hannibal, Will also convinced him this is why he accepted and it was also anticipated by Hannibal, as i already said Will hates Hannibal for what he has done to him and he will do anything to arrest him.Will is also worried for Alana because she’s close to Hannibal  (this is why he would do anything to arrest Hannibal, not only for himself but also to protect the people he loves). The next part of Hannibal’s strategy to frame Chilton will be using the killer who killed someone in the trial as Will’s copycat. Hannibal induced that he must be someone working in Chilton hospital because he would need extremely high knowledge to replicate all of Will’s supposed murders, he killed the judge of Will's trial and the location of the trial is close to the hospital. Knowing this, Hannibal foresaw that Will would try to contact the killer via Freddy and ask him to kill Hannibal. Now what should Hannibal do? His goal is to accuse Chilton, so he will purposely let himself be trapped by Will’s copycat (after he anticipated that Will will send him to kill him). In the meantime he will alert Jack and Alana about his situation to guarantee that he will be safe. Indeed Jack and Alana would come and save him on time. The reason Hacnnibal let Will’s copycat capture him was because he wanted to achieve the following: 1) he wanted to gain further sympathy for Jack and Alana by making himself look like the victim in this situation 2) he wanted to further incriminate Will by making him look more like a killer while also turning Jack and Alana against him for his actions 3) to take Will’s copycat out of the way while also remaining innocent in the eyes of his colleagues. Now Hannibal was gaining more sympathy from Jack and Bloom and at the same time turning them against Will. The difficult part was that even after the killing of Will’s copycat, the judge in charge of Will’s case wasn’t willing to drop the case, so Hannibal needed to postpone the trial. Hannibal needed Will free in order to use him against Chilton, so he killed the judge that was in charge of Will’s trial by making everyone believe that he was killed by the copycat of Will’s. Afterwards Will’s trial was postponed. Now as Hannibal wanted to misdirect the FBI, he had to frame Chilton in a perfect way. After Hannibal had completely rearranged her memories, the stage was ready for Hannibal’s plan to take action. Hannibal used Miriam for two reasons: 1) to clear his name of any suspicion of being the Chesapeake Ripper (especially after Will’s claims that Hannibal was behind his manipulation) and 2) to reinforce the framing of Chilton by creating an eye witness for Chilton’s supposed crimes and not only that, but also to kill Chilton on sight so that the case would be closed for good. Obviously this isn't normal, he runs to leave only to be stopped by Hannibal, but the FBI arrive at the same time. Hannibal stops Chilton from screaming while the FBI rings his doorbell. [Third Layer of Deception]

(Strategy, Planning, Foresight, Manipulation, Deception)

Hannibal’s Contingencies

Hannibal’s Plan and Strategy had 3 layers of complexity in which each layer was coated in a veil of Deception and Misdirection. At the beginning Hannibal was killing people by creating different psychological profiles for the murders, creating the idea that those killings were performed by a random killer (either a copycat, an organ harvester or some unknown individual). So that when the FBI first found the murders they would suspect some unknown killer rather than Hannibal. [First Layer of Deception]
Now even if someone was able to break the first layer of his deception and was able to deduce that all of the previous (seemingly unrelated) murders were in fact related and were the work of a single serial killer (like Will did during the end of season one) then Hannibal would have a second layer of deception. Which would be Will Graham. Hannibal was actually planning to frame Will from the first episodes of the series, by planting physical evidence and psychically driving him to make it look like Will was slowly losing his mind. At the end even if the FBI was able to bypass Hannibal’s first layer of deception, they still wouldn’t find Hannibal but Will in his place. Meaning that the killer behind all the previous (seemingly unrelated) murders would be Will and not Hannibal. [Second layer of Deception]
Now even if someone was able to see through Hannibal’s second layer of deception (which was Will) and manage to see that Will was framed (which is very hard considering that Hannibal’s Frame was so well executed that he was able to make even Will himself believe that he was the killer). Then the FBI still wouldn’t be able to find Hannibal because of Hannibal’s third layer of deception which was Chilton and what make it so impressive is that, even if they saw through Will framing they wouldn't understand why Hannibal (the real killer) really framed Will, it was in order to frame Chilton but the FBI would just conclude that Chilton did that to be innocent. Hannibal's plan to frame Chilton started years ago and was holding it in an attempt to misdirect the FBI if they even got too close. [Third layer of Deception]
Hannibal essentially created 3 backup plans or different layers of complexity that were perfectly connected with each other in order to misdirect the FBI away from him. With each layer being extremely complex on its own. The Framing of Chilton was just the final part of his larger plan to misdirect the FBI, with each plan completely transcending the previous one in terms of scale and connections. The only way for someone to bypass the first two layers was by connecting all the seemingly unrelated elements and even then they wouldn’t be able to find Hannibal but only another contingency waiting for them (with each contingency being harder to connect than the previous one because of scale). Essentially, in order to find the Copycat they’ll need to connect all the unrelated murders to deduce the existence of a single killer behind them. And even if they are able to arrive at this conclusion they will find Will instead of Hannibal. Then in order to find the Chesapeake Ripper they’ll need to connect all of his previous murders of the Ripper’s + all the Copycat murders into a single name that combines all the previous layers together. This contingency was only If the FBI had understood that Will was framed, they would have concluded that someone had framed him and that he must therefore be the killer of the previous cases. They would have realized that he had framed him so that he wouldn't have any suspicions, and in the end, they would have found Chilton. So the killer (Hannibal) is trying to make them think that it's a basic frame (if they saw through Will framing) While it has a completely different objective, which is to use Will Graham to manipulate Gideon/Chilton so that Chilton accuses himself unintentionally. For example, at the trial Chilton accused Will, in prison Will used Gideon, Chilton gave the recording to Jack as if Chilton were accusing Hannibal, then afterwards Gideon no longer accused Hannibal but Chilton when Jack was there, as if Chilton were manipulating Gideon. That, along with the fact that Hannibal had connected the Copycat case with the Chesapeake Ripper case via Gideon, made every element point towards Chilton as the prime suspect. So even after they do that, and Hannibal proved them with even more evidence, they will find Chilton not Hannibal (after Chilton there are no other murders to connect to Hannibal). Basically by doing that Hannibal is perfectly safe from the FBI.
Thus, Hannibal was able to continue his killings while also misdirecting the FBI at the same time, for 4.5 years. Killing dozens of people.
Overview:
Hannibal’s goal: Misdirect the FBI

First Plan/Contingency : Creating random killing with unrelated psychological background so that no one will suspect you

Second Plan/Contingency: connect all of the previous unrelated elements to point to a single person in case someone is able to connect them. (That person is Will aka the Copycat)

Third Plan/Contingency: connect all of your previous elements of the last years to point to a single person in case the FBI is still looking for a killer (That would be Chilton aka the ChR)

Note : this “contingency” are only contingencies in case the FBI is able to see through Hannibal plan, for example if they saw through Will framing and start suspecting Hannibal, Hannibal would probably frame Chilton directly with Miriam lass. This is why Hannibal coverage is insane, into his plan he always makes sure to create a contingency in case they saw through his plan.

Each layer of his plan was designed to lead the FBI away from Hannibal and misdirected their attention about his exploits into another target.

The reason this is a single Plan with 3 different layers of complexity and contingencies is because Hannibal’s strategies were all operating at the same time and not separately and they were all part of his larger plan to avoid the FBI. In addition, they were created by the elements of each other and Hannibal was operating and progressing them at the same time

The Copycat cases = Will Graham

The Chesapeake Ripper case = Chilton

———————————————————————————

1st Plan —> create fake psychological profiles to misdirect the FBI

2nd Plan —> Frame Will in case they ever come too close (if they are able to connect all the previous murders). Hannibal had contingency within contingencies with Miriam lass and Gideon; if the FBI was suspicious he could frame Chilton directly with Miriam lass.

3rd Plan —> Frame Chilton if they ever found out that Will has been manipulated by someone

The timeline of Hannibal’s Planning:

Hannibal started killing after he had already planned to frame Chilton in the far future so that after his framing there will be no more crimes for the FBI to connect with Hannibal. Basically after the Chesapeake Ripper case has been closed with Chilton’s death by the hand of Miriam, Hannibal will essentially be innocent since we don’t even know if he has committed any other crimes outside of the cases he creates.

Chilton is the last part of his plan and the first one that was preparing: Hannibal started preparing for Chilton first -> then for Will -> then he created the random murders.

Hannibal’s preparation order:
1)Avoid the FBI -> 2) Frame Chilton -> 3) Frame Will -> 4) Create unrelated murders

Time of completion:
1). Create unrelated murders -> 2) Frame Will -> 3) Frame Chilton -> 4) Avoid the FBI

The plan that he was firstly preparing was also the latest one to complete. Hannibal created different layers, with the one transcending the previous one in terms of connections and scale (meaning in order to bypass the one you needed to connect all of the previous elements together). Like circles inside of bigger circles.

Hannibal (The ChR case(The Copycat case(RK) ) )

Hannibal created a 3 layered plan so that even if someone was able to come even close to him, they would never be able to find him.

Now, the reason this plan is airtight and keeps Hannibal safe even after the framing of Chilton is because after Chilton there are no further connections between Hannibal and even a single murder. Hannibal, before he even started to operate as the Chesapeake Ripper, had plans to connect every single element that he was going to create until the framing of Chilton with each other. Which means that every crime Hannibal had committed over the last few years was immediately going to be closed with the framing of Chilton. Essentially there will be no more connections and evidence against Hannibal because there would be no more murders to connect him with (no more crimes). Everything that Hannibal had created from the beginning of his plan would be connected and closed with his 3rd and final layer. So Hannibal was perfectly safe after the completion of his plan to avoid the FBI.

(Planning Coverage, Intricacies, Contigenses Concealment, Misdirection)

Other Contingencies

Hannibal also could have created another contingency which wasn’t used in the show. If Hannibal is always using several cards, he might have thought of framing Jack in a case he would need to. He befriended him, was involved in Bella's treatment so had some point of entry in his personal life. He planted discord between Will and Jack, taking away Jack's best bet. He kept Alana engaged and even was the reason for some discord between them, Alana once lost it and shouted how Jack is pushing Will. Miriam lass was Jack crawford agent before disappearing. Jack ends up looking as someone who has done the following : Being obsessive and losing it, Hannibal must have known he is capable of obsession. Pushing Will to breaking. (The law may look at it as deliberately destroying a means for reaching the truth ?) How difficult would it be for Hannibal to make a claim that Jack was pushing Will making it look deliberate. After all he is Will's doctor and Alana would back him up, even other psychiatrists could. Looking in every odd direction and deflecting Being casual about Miriam, then Will, then Beverly.. if Hannibal could link at least one of them like Miriam to Jack it makes Jack vulnerable. Jack was shown to hate Will in season 2 and even in season 1 for what he was accused of, it would make sense if he was the real killer, he would want Will to be framed. Being a behavioral head and having access to psychiatrists and techniques, Jack could have been proved as creating cover.

Hannibal’s Acting Skills

Other than Hannibal’s exceptional strategic deception and concealment, there were also other means that he used in order to deceive the FBI and everyone around him. One example would be his ability to conceal his true nature via his charisma and acting:
Additionally to Hannibal’s strategic misdirection and concealment, Hannibal was also acting throughout the entirety of the series. Hannibal was constantly acting as less intelligent than he really was in order to avoid any suspicion. He didn’t want to reveal to the world his true capabilities so he was always acting only as smart as people thought he was and not as he was in reality to avoid any suspicion. We can see that in S1 E10, when Hannibal’s friends who knew Hannibal for many years (since they were in medical school) knew about Hannibal’s ability to identify the perfumes of the nurses but he didn’t knew about his ability to identify diseases (even though Hannibal could do it as a child). Hannibal never talked or displayed this type of ability in front of his friend. Another instance would be his relationship with Bedelia (his therapist). Even though they knew each other for many years, she knows that Hannibal is always wearing a human vale and that she only has conversations with a version of Hannibal and not the real thing. She knows that she only has conversations with a “version”of himself. Then, in S2 Ep2 we see Hannibal taking the place of Will in the FBI investigation team, where Hannibal is acting exactly as Will would have. Even though he is able to understand the killer to a much higher degree than he admits to the FBI he chooses to keep this information from himself so that he wouldn’t draw any attention to his face. He chooses not to give the FBI his full insight and to not outshine Will. We also have E8 of S2 when Hannibal says that he’s unable to understand the killer and that he needs Will, even though we have seen that Hannibal is able understand someone better than Will (S1 E1, S2 E2), but Hannibal is acting as less competent than Will so that Jack wouldn’t be able to perceive Hannibal’s true potential which would be scary. Hannibal throughout the show had also needed to show weakness on multiple occasions by letting someone catch him or beat him to appear more vulnerable to the rest of the world (S1 E8, S2 E6). Hannibal had created a different persona when he was in the company of people, even Will himself when he was trying to understand the Chesapeake Ripper in S1 E7 knew that the Ripper (Hannibal) had the ability to seem completely normal to the rest of the world thus not a threat. Furthermore, Hannibal was so good at creating a false image that even by the beginning of S2 (at Will’s trial) after his framing of Will, everyone still believed that Will Graham was “the smartest person in the room”, and not Hannibal. They never even considered that Hannibal might be capable of something like that. Overall Hannibal was acting as someone less capable than he really was by hiding his true capabilities when he was operating as Dr Hannibal Lecter. He did that to create a safe feeling for those around him and to make them trust him and sympathize with him. He never gave anyone any reason to suspect that he might indeed be more than he appears to be. The only 2 people that Hannibal allowed to see even a fraction of his true abilities were Bedelia and Will, and after taking a glance at his true nature they immediately found him dangerous and a monster. It’s strange to think that Jack, Bloom, Will and many others already consider Hannibal to be superior to them in most cases (Alana was praising Hannibal to Jack, says that he taught her a lot / Jack was considering himself layman in Hannibal’s presence / and Will (even though he didn’t believe in psychotherapy) thought that Hannibal was the only one who could help him) they think that without Hannibal even revealing to them his true nature and capabilities. After they all witnessed his true nature they will all consider Hannibal the devil himself and someone that can’t be apprehended. Hannibal was essentially acting weaker than he was throughout the series both intellectually and physically. (Acting Skills, Charisma, Concealment)

Hannibal knows like no one else how to instigate a human being. Using Anthony Dimmond, he exploited Bedelia's insecurity about her own destiny to strengthen his connection with her. First present a next target, make your victim think you actions are predictable, but break your victim's expectations and don't kill that target, but take him to a position that only your victim could be in, make him feel insecure, in then, give a speech that fits perfectly into your victim's destiny, make her remember when her broke your trust and her guilt, but at the point of total cutting off with your victim, surprise her with a second chance by fulfilling her previous expectations, killing the target; make her rethink her thoughts and reflect on her subjection, and thus, consolidate her control over her victim.

S3 Ep 6

- Hannibal draws the streets of Florence, wishing he could draw them from memory. He decides to leave Florence. Jack and Will arrive at Bedelia's house, only she's pretending to be someone else. she's taken drugs so they won't say anything about Hannibal, now why?  Let’s firstly talk about her and her relationship with Hannibal . On a surface level, these things make it appear like she knows more than she actually does. But when she said that he’s not honest and referred to his “person suit,” or “human veil,” she also said that “I have conversations with a version of [Hannibal] and hope that the actual [Hannibal] gets what he needs.” She really doesn’t know anything except what he tells her. She’s a smart lady—Hannibal wouldn’t be interested in a therapist who was anything but—but there’s a very long leap from “I know you’re not telling me all the truth” to “You’re the Chesapeake Ripper and you eat people.” When she says, “I see enough of you to see the truth of you,” we know this is dramatic irony; in any case, she wouldn’t need to say these words or discuss his honesty at all if she really did know the truth of him, because there would be nothing left to hide between them. The main reason I think she doesn’t have any advanced knowledge of him is because we are given only one significant insight into her personality, but we are reminded of this characteristic of hers in nearly every single scene with her. And that is that she is profoundly moral in regards to her profession, which she takes very seriously. She reinforces those boundaries with Hannibal at nearly every turn, starting with refusing multiple invitations to his dinner table. She seems to have a real moral dedication to her profession, and she advises him accordingly. Therefore there’s no way that she could have full knowledge of and condone Hannibal’s murderous and cannibalistic hobbies as it was shown in S1 when she eats Hannibal food while in S3 she doesn’t as she connected the dots. I think he maintains his relationship with her out of affection and admiration, even though it’s possible she disappointed him as in season 3 Hannibal says that he wanted to eat her. Based on his relationship with Alana, he obviously doesn’t require a person to be violent in order to care for her. One of the more perverse things about Hannibal is that he’s attracted to people who are dangerous to him. Will, Abigail, Jack—even Freddie—they all possess the power to destroy him, and he just can’t leave them alone. I think this is true to some extent with Bedelia as well. I imagine he refused to be referred to another therapist for several reasons. His very real interest in her was one of them, and it seems the only way he could actively keep her as a part of his life was in this role. They clearly aren’t “psychiatrist friends.” He really does use therapy, as well—it’s the one place he can talk through his thoughts in the most honest and most protected way, to help maintain clarity in his own thinking. He also needs a therapist in a practical sense, to sort of “try out” the rationale of his evasions and half-truths to see what will fly and what won’t, like when he’s discussing madness as a medicine for modern life. Bedelia basically looks him in the eye and says, “Bullshit,” so he knows that isn’t going to fly.  After her attack he’s vetted her now and knows just how far he can push her, and the fact that she’s lied to protect him at least twice now implicates her to some extent and gives him some power over her. If he did do something really criminal when he killed the patient who attacked her, as it seems he must have, the fact that she covered for him makes her an accessory to the crime at the very least. They each possess knowledge of the other that gives them power as well as takes it away, fostering trust. It’s rather like how Abigail’s knowledge that Hannibal was the man on the phone puts her on more equal footing with him, but it’s balanced by his knowledge that she killed Nick Boyle, or when Hannibal convinced Will to keep his silence about Abigail killing Nick Boyle and Hannibal helping her hide the body. By giving a little power in each of these instances, Hannibal gains significant power in return. They’re all deals with the devil. He has a similar deal in place with Bedelia, which makes her unable to rid herself of him when she stops seeing patients. She protects Hannibal in spite of not approving of whatever it is Hannibal is doing with Will. She pretty much says so in her next scene with Hannibal, when she faithfully reports to him that Jack came questioning. Although we don’t really know all that happened during her attack, it doesn’t appear that she needs to protect herself, so her need to protect Hannibal appears to arise out of a loyalty to him as her patient. So Hannibal takes it as well, when he says, “You protect your patient from Jack Crawford, but I can’t protect mine
(VSI, emotional understanding)

Hannibal would know all about the periodic table of elements and state that he also knows the chaos theory. Chaos theory originated in the 1890s, with the work of Henri Poincaré, but it is still relevant today in tackling problems that are crucial to society: the prediction of extreme weather events, the pathologies of cardiac variability, the spread of epidemics... Chaos theory is a scientific theory linked to mathematics and physics that studies the behavior of dynamic systems sensitive to initial conditions, a phenomenon generally illustrated by the butterfly effect. - In many dynamic systems, minute changes in initial conditions lead to rapidly divergent evolutions, making long-term prediction impossible. Although these are deterministic systems, whose future behavior is determined by the initial conditions, without any intervention of chance, they are unpredictable (at least in detail) because the initial conditions cannot be known with infinite precision. - Chaos theory is primarily a branch of dynamical systems theory and ordinary differential equation theory. The basics of this theory (e.g. differential flow, Cauchy problem) needed to approach the notion of chaos are not very difficult, undergraduate level. For the basic notions of chaos theory (e.g. strictly positive Lyapunov exponent, harmonic splitting cascade, Feigenbaum constants, strange attractors, fractal dimension... ). Then, of course, it gets as complicated as you like, as in all other disciplines (e.g. Ruelle-Takens-Sauer folding theorem, symplectic geometry...). In short, the BASICS of chaos theory are fairly straightforward and accessible at undergraduate or graduate level. Molly recovers from surgery and guesses that Hannibal is behind her wound. She's angry at Jack, who knew that both she and Walter were at risk but Will reminds her that he isn't exempt from fault. Will graham psychoanalysis (not complete)

On the surface, Will appears fragile, introverted, and emotionally vulnerable. His soft-spoken nature, frequent social withdrawal, and apparent discomfort in social situations give him an air of fragility that evokes both empathy and concern from those around him. However, this exterior fragility masks a profound inner darkness that emerges through his extraordinary and often harrowing ability to empathize with criminals. Will's hyper-empathy allows him to understand and internalize the thoughts and feelings of those he hunts, which not only aids in his criminal profiling but also draws him into a dangerous psychological abyss. At their first meeting, Hannibal will directly notice that Will speaks strangely, is a little uncomfortable, which can often indicate autism, for example. He will then directly notice that Will doesn't like eye contact and infer that Will, who is possibly someone with strong empathy (reinforced by the fact that he's a profiler) is able to make very quick connections and understand what people have using cold reading. Will then confirms this, that he doesn't like it because he learns too much and makes involuntary connections as if he can't control them. These associations terrify him, which is why he builds fortresses to protect himself. From this conversation, Hannibal is already able to understand that Will has two sides to him, one fragile, uncomfortable, and one who frequents these associations of ideas, who is terrified but who puts up with them. Will works against the criminals, but at the same time is on the side of the police, he wants to frequent these abysses while being on the side of the good guys, and we see the same pattern when he becomes a professor, allowing him to fully analyze killers and crime scenes from a theoretical point of view, while being on the good side. To test this, Hannibal decides to call Garret Jacob Hobbs to manipulate him into killing his family so that when Will arrives he'll be forced to kill him to save Abigail. He felt pleasure in killing Garret, except that there are several possible reasons: he felt it because he likes to kill, he felt it because he saved someone from a killer or he felt it because he had put himself in Garret's head and therefore felt what he felt when he killed This act opened the door he wanted to leave closed. In episode 2, Will almost killed Eldon, he thought about it but didn't, his nature came out. Will feels bad about it, but Hannibal understands that this side of Will is his true side, that he can't help it and is just trying to convince himself that it's wrong so he can be normal, so Hannibal comforts him by using an analogy to his situation, saying that God must enjoy himself too if he kills so much, anyone would walk away on hearing this justification but not Will because that's what he wanted to hear. Will tries to be normal, in this relationship, he will try to go out with Alana but won't be able to, because he's not normal, he can't be understood, that's why Hannibal knows what he can do, he knows that Will knows that he's the only one who can understand him and also accept him. Will has never been loved by anyone except his father, and Hannibal guesses that at school he was the odd one out, so by manipulating Abigail he can manipulate Will, who for the first time has someone he loves and must protect, which is why Will agrees to cover up Abigail's murder To gain acceptance of his nature, Hannibal will manipulate Will into believing that he has killed Abigail, while at the same time framing him so that he develops a hatred for him, with the aim of forcing him to attack Hannibal, even to the point of trying to kill him. But at the same time, he knows that Hannibal is the only person who accepts and understands him, so he can't kill him, knowing that after episode 7 Will is no longer in prison. In this episode, he tries to kill Hannibal but doesn't for these reason, after that he kills randall tiers with his hands, try to kill freddie lounds, he completely accept his dark self in mizumono in which he call Hannibal to tell him that Jack is coming.

Will's person suit is a complex amalgamation of his true self and the facade he presents to navigate his world. Unlike Hannibal, who removes his person suit in a dramatic fashion, Will’s suit is woven with threads of self-deception and denial, making it difficult to discern where his true nature ends and his constructed persona begins. This complexity is evident in his appearance and behavior: his Season 1 plaids and scruff suggest a man indifferent to social norms, yet his transformation in Season 2 into a more polished individual signals a deeper entanglement with his darker self, especially under Hannibal's influence. Unlike Hannibal, who meticulously crafted his public image as a respectable surgeon and socialite while secretly indulging his dark urges, Will’s facade is entangled in ambiguity, making it challenging to distinguish between his true self and the aspects he hides. In the first season, Will appears vulnerable, introverted, and socially awkward, with his scruffy appearance and casual attire suggesting a lack of concern for societal norms. His exceptional empathy, which allows him to understand killers, is presented as a gift he uses to save lives, positioning him as a savant-like figure dedicated to justice. However, moments of stark contrast emerge, such as his aggression towards Hannibal and Jack, or his selective malice towards Freddie and Alana, hinting at a deeper, darker nature. This raises the question of whether Will’s pursuit of killers is solely about saving lives or if he has convinced himself of this noble purpose to justify darker, latent urges. His shift in appearance from the disheveled look of season one to a more polished demeanor in season two, and his reluctance to revert to his former style, suggest that his initial look was itself a crafted persona, hiding a more deliberate self. Will’s use of glasses as a barrier, particularly during significant events like his first meeting with Hannibal and his first ‘murder’ of Hobbs, symbolizes his attempts to distance himself from his actions and true nature, yet this barrier is often breached. His vocation in law enforcement allows him to explore his darker impulses under the guise of legitimacy and safety. The series flirts with various psychological labels for Will, such as being on the autism spectrum or unstable, which he uses to mask his deeper identity as a repressed killer. His attempts at normalcy, like his marriage to Molly or his relationship with Alana, reflect a yearning to belong and be seen as normal, though these are ultimately revealed as veneers over his true self. Hannibal and Bedelia insights into Will, often communicated through metaphors of clothing and armor, underscore the idea that Will’s public persona is a fragile construct, easily stripped away in the presence of Hannibal, revealing a man at peace with his darkness. The series’ use of props and attire, such as Randall Tier’s beast suit or Hannibal’s dramatic doffing of his personal suit, further illustrate the thematic importance of identity and facade. In the epilogue, Will’s transformation into a man who embraces his dark impulses, dressed in formals and devoid of his glasses, symbolizes his acceptance of his true self, free from the constraints of his earlier personas. Ultimately, Will’s journey is one of self-discovery, peeling back the layers of his person suit to reveal a complex, conflicted individual whose true nature lies somewhere between the fragile empath and the latent killer

Hannibal’s Mind Palace and Cognition

“My Memory Palace is large and vast, even by medieval standards. The entrance hall is the Norman Chapel in Palermo. Severe, beautiful and timeless, with a single reminder of mortality; a skull carved into the ground.” ~ Hannibal Lecter, NBC series.
Hannibal’s Mind Palace is described as vast, even for medieval standards and is in this place that Hannibal stores all of his memories. Hannibal’s Mind Palace is so detailed and well structured, to the point that Hannibal himself states that if they ever captured him he could essentially “live there”, as a form of a different reality for him. Hannibal’s MP (the building established as the Palaces lobby)
is based on the Cappella Palatina in the Palazzo Reale, which is based on a real structure in the city of Palermo.

Hannibal's Mental Palace is outlined as a structure of considerable dimensions, remarkably expansive, even in comparison to medieval standards, the environment where Hannibal stores his memories and memories, as evidenced in the literary canon and in the TV series. Given these assertions, it would be correct to infer similarities between the size of the structure of the two versions. Through the measurements and data of the structure, we can quantify the amount of detailed information that Lecter's visual-spatial perception precisely conceives. The building is a splendid example of Norman architecture. It features a ceiling adorned in golden mosaics, with figures and scenes of a religious nature, especially biblical, in addition to the colorful marble columns highlighted around the chapel. Finally, its architectural apogee portrays the richness of cultural fusion in the region. Hannibal is able to visualize the entire interior of the Norman Chapel of Palermo with extreme clarity and depth (including all the details of the structure). Hannibal can essentially visualize 1800 sqm along with 18 m of height and 16 m in width. So Hannibal can visualize 32.400 m3  of dimensional space in his mind along with all the details of the interior.

Outside of all the different objects inside his MP that Hannibal can perfectly visualize he can also do the same with the pictures on the walls of his MP. Hannibal can visualize 424 different mosaics, which are used to create 314 different images. Even the smallest mosaics are composed of at least 360 pieces or tiles. (insane Visual acuity)

Which means that Hannibal can visualize at least  150.000+ visual information at the same time. If we use the highest estimates this number can go as far as more than 5.000.000 different visual informations at the same time. The average estimate would be around 1.000.000 visual information. (Just for the mosaics)

In the Script of Season 3, Episode 2 - Primavera, it’s stated that the Norman Chapel is just where his memory palace begins. Beyond it, far and complex, is the vast structure of his mind. It contains a thousand rooms and miles of corridors.

So Hannibal can visualize:

1. 32.400 m3 of dimensional space
2. Every object and detail inside this 3D space
3. At least 150.000+ different visual information for the mosaics alone
4. A thousand rooms and miles of corridors beyond the Norman Chapel

(VSI, Visual-Spatial Intelligence, Visual Perception, Dimensional Awareness)

Cognition:

Hannibal can also operate in his MP and in the real world at the same time, since he was able to converse with Will in his MP while also being able to navigate himself through his prison cell in the real world at the same time (S3 Ep13). He was basically talking to Will in his MP and in the real world at the same time. Due to the detail of the architecture, in terms of fidelity to reality, Hannibal perceives the material and imaginary world as distinct, although identical, providing him with a new space as a home. We know that Hannibal’s MP is so detailed that Hannibal himself perceives it as a different reality, stating that "If I'm ever apprehended, my memory palace will serve as more than a mnemonic system. I will live there” and we can also see him interacting with objects in his MP like he was in the real world. Essentially, it’s like Hannibal can operate on two different realities at once, the one being his mental construct and the other being the physical world, without the one interfering with the other.

(WMI, Multitasking, Divergence Thinking, Abstract Reasoning, Cognition)

Hannibal is also able to sense and perceive things even when he’s in his MP. For example in S2 Ep 7, Hannibal was able to hear music in the halls of his MP (which he had probably stored from some orchestra he had visited in the past) with clarity and detail. Hannibal outside of visual information can also store auditory (S2 E7), smells (S2 E2), and tastes (S2 E10), in his MP. He is able to perceive his MP through his 5 senses, making his memories seem as real as the real world. He is also able to sense the environment of the real world even when he’s in his MP. He was able to sense Will’s presence in the room while being in his MP (probably by smelling him).
(Perception, Memory, Cognition)

Hannibal’s Split Brain Syndrome Theory:

It was stated by Will Graham at the beginning of Season 3, that Hannibal “can follow several trains of thought at the same time without being distracted by any of them” (we see that when he’s operating in the real world and at his MP at the same time). He also goes on to say that one of those trains of thought is always about his amusement. By “trains of thought” he means that Hannibal can essentially run several different thought processes about different cognitive tasks at the same time by dividing the tasks into different parts of his brain. Several = more than two, so Hannibal can basically run 3+ different thought processes at the same time. This is an example of robust cognition. He can divide the tasks he wants to compute between the two of his hemispheres, and simultaneously run them, without each distracting one another. As it’s stated one of his hemispheres is always for his amusement, whilst the other registers real time and computes real time works. Meaning, whilst one of his hemispheres is endeavoring to finish his real time correspondences, the other hemisphere could brainstorm and generate ideas that aren’t directly connected with his real time correspondences.

An example would be:
One of his hemispheres would be registering his patients convo and generating his responses , whilst the other hemisphere assimilates his observations of his
patient all at once.

Overall his split brain syndrome gives him crazy multi-tasking abilities that can even allow him to operate in his MP and in the real world at the same time, calculating and perceiving different cognitive problems simultaneously. The ability to run several train of thought at the same time without being distracted by any of them is completely beyond the realms of reality, it is not possible for any human to achieve at the same time two important task, even if you try it won’t be efficient due to the cognitive load and the fact that you might associate information of the first task to the second, but Hannibal can not only run at least 3 thought so 3 task, but he can do it efficiently.  (Cognition, Multitasking, Faster Thinking, PSI)

Other Rooms in the Mind Palace:
As highlighted several times in all media, Lecter's Mental Palace consists of several rooms. Going beyond the entrance hall, (in that case the Norman Chapel), we have Hannibal's office — its appearance is elegantly designed — artistic, refined and symbolic, expressing the doctor's tastes and sophisticated nature. With an art nouveau style inside, the office has an extensive personal library, artistic frames, Danish furniture and a color palette of gray and red, oscillating between the owner's apathy and violence. The exterior of the office resembles that of a historic building.

The content recorded within the mental palace ranges from literary-academic knowledge, profiles, psychological and medicinal diaries, affective memories and sensitive memories, that is, views, sounds, odors, flavors and sensations. We often see Lecter appreciate good music in his private world, instrumentals and vocals, as well as remembering the odors of chemical, environmental, culinary substances, and those of his acquaintances, since he recognized the smell of Graham's shaving lotion after years.
The books from the doctor's personal library also exist in his Mental Palace, thus, it can be deduced that Hannibal keeps all the knowledge in his library in the confines of his palace, a fact that is consistent with Lecter's previous statements about prioritizing his comfort and fun. The books are quite diverse and differ between their genres, below is a list of these titles: https://weartherude.tumblr.com/post/56716405878/hannibals-library-contains-hundreds-of-rare-and/amp

Hannibal’s Knowledge:
Hannibal throughout the series displays his extensive knowledge in various fields and subjects such as:

Humanitarian Sciences
* Music (S1 E8)
* Literature (S3 E1)
* Philosophy (S2 E11)
* Classical Studies (S1 E1, S3 E3)
* Psychology (S1 E1, S3 E9, S2 E9)
* Poetry (S2 E13, S3 E1, E3)
* Medicine (S1 E10)
* Surgery (S1 E7)
* Anatomy (S1 E6, S2 E13)

Social Sciences
* History (S1 E8, S3 EP 5)
* Architecture (S1 E1, S2 E13)
* Philology (S3 E1, E2)
* Tradition (S2 E8, S2 E10)

Natural Sciences
* Biology (S1 E2)
* Neurology (S1 E2, E5)
* Botany (S1 E4)
* Zoology (S2 E9)
* Chemistry (S2 E4)
* Astro Physics (S3 E7)
* Particle Physics (S3 E7)
* Mathematics (S3 E7)

Additional Skills and Hobbies : (all of the following subjects are performed on a highly proficient level)
* Cooking (S1 E7)
* Going to the Opera (S1 E7, S2 E11)
* Organizing Dinner Parties (S1 E7, S2 EP 6)
* Composing Music (S1 E8)
* Playing Instruments (S1 E8, S2 EP 6)
* Painting/Sketching/Drawing (S1 E1, S3 E2)
* Exercising Calligraphy (S2 E13)
* Solving Mathematical Equations to reverse time (S3 E7)
* Studying Different Traditions (S3 E1)
* Visiting his Mind Palace (S2 E7, S3 E13)

Keep in mind that if Hannibal does something, he wants to do it perfectly, to completely master the subject and you will see why just after.
Hannibal interview
Hannibal interview give us a lot of new information about Hannibal, which are obviously canon to the show
Hannibal Post Mortem - Mads Mikkelsen
Hannibal is stated by mads to see the beauty in what everyone sees horror, this is what he saw in Will.

Mads Mikkelsen Interview - Hannibal

Hannibal is man of taste, a man of details, nothing is left to chance, it’s the perfectiveness. Hannibal loves literature, cooking, music, history… everything which is not banal.
Mads Mikkelsen Breaks Down His Most Iconic Characters | GQ  (6.26 to 9.18)

Hannibal is a different animal, he can be impulsive but it’s a choice if he does it, he is in complete control. He sees the world differently than what people do, he’s like a fallen angel, he sees the opposite, he sees the loves where we see something horrifying, he sees beauty when we see something very ugly. For Hannibal life is more interesting and fantastic when it’s on the threshold to death. Hannibal is way too intelligent for anyone, Mads states that he doesn't know how he learned so many languages, cooking style, play the theremin and shoot bow and arrow to perfection. Compared to Will Graham who has no control of his empathy, Hannibal has complete control over it. It was shown in the whole show that in most cases Hannibal understands the Killer better than Will do (or they have the same understanding) and Hannibal has the ability to perfectly visualize place + he stated in episode 1 of season 2 to investigate like Will graham, therefore we can assume that he has Will empathy ability but with complete control over it.  (VSI, EQU, EQP, STP)

Hannibal: Mads Mikkelsen "Dr. Hannibal Lecter" Season 2 Premiere On Set Interview Part 1 of 2

Hannibal enjoy every seconds of his life, he is always trying to find new opportunity in every corner, if something fatal our eyes happen today it might turn our world but it doesn't turn his his world, he doesn’t see it as a problem but as a challenge

McLean 2015 - The Art and Making of Hannibal, The Television Series.
Hannibal is stated to be the most charismatic killer in the world
Hannibal is also stated to be omniscient, he obviously isn’t but this emphasis on his ability to see through people, sense their thoughts, that he is always aware of surroundings and it also shows how high his knowledge is.

Bryan Fuller: Hannibal Lecter is unique in his crazy. He’s not a psychopath, because he experiences regret. And he is not a sociopath, because he experiences empathy. So he is unique in his crazy, and that gives him a higher sensibility than just a mortal man ... one of the things that we talked about in our first meeting was not so much about playing Hannibal as the cannibal psychiatrist, as previously portrayed by other actors, but more like Lucifer and how he was a dark angel who had this affinity for mankind and a fascination with the human condition
